            Chapter 1

            UIM Progression Guide — Chapter 1: Tutorial Island to Desert Treasure

            Nov 6, 2026 · @Marcos

            How to read this guide

            This guide is written for Ultimate Ironmen from the first tick, at a comfortable pace, and it never assumes a bank. What it optimises is comfort and safety: no tick manipulation (Chapter 4's fast track is the one exception), the fewest trips, slots and risky deaths, and training that quest XP would give you later left to the quests. It is not the fewest-hours route. Its mechanics were last checked against the wiki on 23 November 2026, and its coin figures are estimates, so keep about 15% over any target before a big purchase. Comfort and safety come first because a UIM has nothing behind its inventory: one careless death or one overfull trip can cost hours of items, while a slower but safe route costs only minutes. Where the guide picks the slower option, it says why, so you can judge for yourself whether the faster one is worth it.

            Ground rules

            1. There is no bank. Every item lives in your inventory, your worn equipment, or one of the storages in the glossary below. A step never says "grab from the bank". A UIM can't deposit, withdraw or note anything at a bank. A bank booth still turns notes back into items for you (as many as your free slots hold; for an exact number, fill the other slots first or pay Phials in Rimmington 5 gp an item), which the Entrana kit in 1.3 uses to unnote its fish at the Falador east bank, but it never stores anything. Services that send items to your bank do nothing for a UIM either, so no step relies on one.
            2. Count item types, not quantities. Coins, runes, feathers, arrow shafts, noted loot and tokens each take one slot however many you hold. Twenty-eight unstackable items fill you up. This is why the slot counts move when you pick up a quest item but not when your coins grow, and why the guide takes the stackable or noted form wherever there is a choice: the arrow shafts from Lumbridge are about 1,500 items in one slot, and the Wintertodt Reward Cart pays out logs, gems, ores, herbs and fish as notes, one slot per type. The opposite case is a step that needs many free slots at once. Sleeping Giants needs 20 empty slots for a short time when you search Kovac's crate, so the guide moves it to a moment when a planned death has emptied your inventory.
            3. Just-in-time over pre-buying. If an item is cheap and sold or spawned near where you use it, the guide gets it there. It never buys a quest item thirty steps early to hold it. An item bought early costs a slot on every step between the purchase and its use, and for a UIM a slot is the only storage there is. In this chapter, for example, the silk for Dragon Slayer I and Ghosts Ahoy comes from the Al Kharid silk trader when you pass him, not from the Ardougne stall weeks earlier, and the nettles for Recipe for Disaster are picked next to the place that uses them.
            4. Finish what you start. A quest item that would sit in your inventory for many steps is a cost. This guide completes short quests in one visit where it can. A started quest often hands you items that only its later steps take back, so leaving it half done means carrying them. Where a quest can't be finished in one visit, the guide waits until it can: the last Eadgar's Ruse hand-in in 1.4 is done in a single trip to the trolls, and that frees the two slots its items have held since the start.
            5. Wear your tools. Equipment slots are free storage. From 5 Attack the steel axe goes in the weapon slot; the forestry kit, ghostspeak amulet and warm clothing are all worn. The steel axe needs 5 Attack to wield, which is why it rides in your inventory until then. Some worn items do two jobs: the clue hunter gloves and boots you dig up near Ardougne also count as warm clothing at Wintertodt, and the forestry kit keeps Forestry items such as leaves and bark in your cape slot.
            6. Comfortable pace. No tick manipulation, no world-hopping beyond what a step names, no 1.5t anything. Optional speed-ups are marked Optional. The main text assumes you skip every Optional note, so leaving one out never leaves you short later. Chapter 4, the road to the max cape, is the one exception: its main track is the fastest method a UIM can use, tick manipulation included, and its Easier option paragraphs keep to this rule.
            7. Deaths are planned or avoided. You keep nothing on death: on a normal (PvM) death every item drops to your deathpile, and unnoted food and potions vanish from it. Locking an item with a Trouver parchment doesn't change this for a UIM, so the rune pouch drops with everything else, which the planned deaths for Recruitment Drive and Entrana rely on. The Chronicle goes further: it can't be dropped, so instead of landing in the pile it is destroyed by every death, planned or not. Its teleport charges are saved, and Diango in Draynor sells it back for 300 gp. Each planned death in this guide says where you buy it back; after any other death, buy it the next time you pass Draynor. A death to a player in the Wilderness is different: most of what you carry goes to the killer, and even a locked rune pouch loses the runes inside it. The guide only ever dies on purpose, and says so. The reason is that a UIM can't keep any item through death, and the Protect Item prayer gives no benefit, so every death moves your whole inventory to the tile where you fell, where only you can see it. Inside an instance the items are placed outside it instead, which is why Fight Arena in 1.3 tells you to walk out rather than risk General Khazard. A planned death turns this into short-term storage: in 1.3 you die in Falador Castle before the Rogues' Den and again before Entrana, and to the rock crabs outside Rellekka before Peer the Seer, each time because the next step needs an empty inventory and nothing worn.

            Before every trip

            Check these before you leave a location. They are the protocol behind the rules above.

            • Recount your inventory against the 28 slots before leaving, and again after any reward, drop or delivery. Each unstackable item takes one slot. Some rewards need free space at the moment you claim them: Misthalin Mystery's three gems are handed over only if you have three empty slots.
            • Confirm no other deathpile is active. A second death does not restart the first pile's 60 minutes of logged-in time. Note the time you die, so you know when the pile runs out. Items from the second death last only for what is left of the first pile's hour, so a second death late in a trip can leave you minutes rather than an hour to collect everything.
            • Before a planned death, check the death setting of every container you carry. The wiki's UIM item management page gives the rune pouch, seed box, herb sack, essence pouches, bolt pouch and tackle box each an "always empty ... on death" setting; with it off, the contents stay inside the container in the pile, and with it on they are dropped beside it.
            • If another player has attacked you, log out or use a rejuvenation pool before a planned death: until that flag clears, the player gets the kill credit and your pile isn't hidden from them. The attack counts even if that player never deals the killing blow, so a planned death right after a player has hit you is not a safe one.
            • Keep the looting bag out of planned deathpile cycles, and never destroy it outside the Wilderness: the Ferox Enclave doesn't count, and the contents are lost for good. Chapter 1 never uses one, so the easiest way to follow this is to leave the looting bag alone until a step asks for it.
            • Treat storage as limited, not as a bank. Use the tool leprechaun, Reward Cart or costume room only for the item types the step names. The costume room in particular won't take a second copy of an item, and as a UIM you can't take any piece back out until the whole set is stored, so put a set in only when you hold all of it.
            • If an Item Retrieval Service holds your items, collect them before any boss, quest fight or Wilderness trip. Any unsafe death while they are stored deletes them. The stored items have no expiry date, which makes them easy to forget: the danger is your next unsafe death, not the clock.
            • Finish each trip with the handoff done: items consumed, delivered, worn or dropped, as the step says.

            How the steps are written

            Each section is split into blocks, one per stretch of the map. A block opens with a short note on what you carry through it, then its steps follow in play order. Each step is written as one continuous walkthrough: where to go, who to talk to, what to pick up, what to wear, and what to throw away the moment it has done its job. Inventory management is part of the instructions, not a separate checklist. A coin estimate closes each block; times are left out, because they depend on your pace. Read a whole step before you start it: the warnings and the drop instructions often come at the end, and some of them change what you do at the start.

            Each block also opens with a route card, so you can check it before you set off:

            • Gate in: what must already be true: levels, quests, items and coins.
            • Carry in: what you bring and roughly how many of your 28 slots it takes.
            • Exit: what is done, held and earned when the block ends, with the coin estimate.
            • Carries forward: the items that cross into later blocks, and where they are used.

            Blocks with fights where a death is unsafe add a Deathbank check line, and long drop grinds an If it doesn't drop line that says when to stop and come back. In the web version, the Continue where I left off button jumps to the first step you haven't ticked, or to the top of its block when you haven't started that block yet, so you see its route and kit first. Ticking a block ticks all its steps, and ticking every step ticks the block. Each action step ends with a Done when line (short notes between steps don't have one): the condition to tick it and move on. The cards and these lines summarise the text around them; the walkthrough is still the instruction. The guide does not trade route efficiency for neatness: the order of the blocks is the order that keeps travel and slots lowest. The slot counts assume you follow the text exactly. If yours is higher, the difference is usually an item a step told you to drop, so check the last few drops before you look for a bigger problem.

            Storage glossary (Chapter 1)

            Inventory + equipmentStart28 slots + worn gearEverything else in this table exists to free these slots
            Reclaimable XP lamps (Veos)X Marks the Spot, Client of KourendQuest XP lampsDestroy the lamp, reclaim it later from Veos. Costs nothing
            Tool leprechaunAny farming patchFarming tools, buckets, compostStores tools and notes harvested herbs and saplings; won't note logs
            Wintertodt Reward CartFirst Wintertodt game with 500+ pointsUp to 8,000 unclaimed rewardsLoot is rolled when you search, not when you earn it
            DeathpileAny timeEverything you carried, except unnoted food and potions; the Chronicle and rainbow scarf are destroyed60 minutes, counted only while logged in
            Table / dropAny timeA few items for a short taskTable: gone 10 minutes after you place the item (others see it after the first minute); ground: gone 3 minutes after you drop it
            Player-owned houseDaddy's HomeFurniture now; magic wardrobe at 42 and armour case at 46 ConstructionSection 1.3 onwards
            Magic wardrobe (costume room)42 Construction, after the graceful outfit in 1.3The graceful outfitA UIM can only take graceful back out when the full set is stored
            Looting bagSection 1.228 tradeable stacksFilled only in the Wilderness or Ferox Enclave; emptied only by dying or destroying it in a Wilderness PvP area. Destroying it in Ferox Enclave, within two tiles of the Wilderness ditch, or outside the Wilderness deletes the contents
            Last Man Standing cofferAny time (Ferox Enclave, free-to-play, no requirements)Coins only, up to 262,143,000 gpDeposits and withdrawals in multiples of 1,000 gp, at any time; Chapter 1 doesn't need it, but it is the safe place for coins before any Wilderness trip
            Deathbank (Item Retrieval Service)Hespori at 65 Farming (Chapter 2); the Volcanic Mine's Petrified Pete returns what you lose in a death there for 150 numuliteEverything you carriedAny unsafe death while items are stored loses them

            Read the table from top to bottom as the order in which storage arrives. Early in 1.1 almost everything rides in your inventory: the Veos lamps and the tool leprechaun each take only a few item types, and the Reward Cart, your first large store, opens with your first Wintertodt game of 500 or more points. The short-term rows behave differently on the ground. A tradeable item you drop shows to other players after one minute and is gone two minutes later, while an untradeable one lasts three minutes, a timer that pauses while you are logged out. A deathpile is hidden from other players for its whole hour unless another player has attacked you. The deathbanks differ in cost: Arno at Hespori charges 25,000 gp to return your items, while Petrified Pete takes numulite, which you earn by handing fossils to the Fossil Collector.

            Chapter 1 at a glance

            Chapter 1 has four destinations, each with a storage milestone, so the account never carries more than it needs.

            SectionDestinationUIM milestone addedWhat changes most
            1.1 Tutorial to WintertodtEarly quests, 50 Firemaking, warm clothing, long WintertodtWintertodt Reward Cart used as deferred storageNo Entrana trip, silk money in timed batches, quest items collected only when used
            1.2 Fishing and thievingRooftops to 40 Agility, Tempoross, Trawler, barbarian fishing, blackjacking to 55 ThievingTempoross reward pool as deferred storage; POH in PollnivneachCoins are the only stockpile; blackjacking stops near 55 Thieving because the rogue outfit needs the 1.3 armour case
            1.3 Money, quests and the road to Fossil IslandMTA, regional quest sweeps, the graceful outfit from Canifis, Bone VoyageCostume room and armour case at 46 Construction, the rogue outfit, about 1.4m gp of blackjacking, rune pouch and Bones to Peaches from the Mage Training Arena, a ring of dueling; three planned deathpilesEach regional sweep finishes the quests it starts; quests whose items would ride for weeks wait for 1.4; Entrana quests done in one emptied-inventory trip; Sleeping Giants moved on to the start of 1.4
            1.4 Farming, Desert Treasure, Chapter 2 money62 Farming at Tithe, Desert Treasure, money for Chapter 2Reward Cart batch split for early Herblore, seed box before the rest, log basket, blackjacking to 85 Thieving for the Chapter 2 money, Hespori deathbank once a seed dropsEarth orbs replaced by blackjacking to 85 Thieving; every Wilderness and Abyss step done inside a planned deathpile; jewellery made a few pieces at a time

            All four sections are written below. Chapters 2 and 3 follow in the same format.

            1.1: Tutorial Island up to and including Wintertodt

            Section 1.1 ends with 85 Firemaking, 60 Woodcutting, 30 Construction, four warm items worn, a house in Rimmington and most of your Wintertodt loot waiting in the Reward Cart. Your standing kit through the section is small on purpose: coins, runes, knife, tinderbox, spade, hammer and a steel axe that moves to your weapon slot at 5 Attack. Everything else is picked up for the step that uses it and dropped once that step is done. The slot counts in this section include the arrow-shaft stack from Lumbridge; take one off each count if you skip the shafts.

            Tutorial Island, Lumbridge and Draynor

            This block takes you from a fresh account to a Draynor kit of about 18 items, with 15 Firemaking, around 19 Woodcutting (11 without the arrow shafts), around 7 Thieving and four quests started or finished. The kit you leave with is: coins, the air and mind rune stacks, a jug of water, one bucket, tinderbox, knife, spade, hammer, steel axe, small fishing net, air talisman, the X Marks the Spot clue, the arrow-shaft stack (if you make it) and four jugs of wine. Worn: leather boots, leather gloves, ghostspeak amulet, forestry kit, bronze sword and wooden shield.

            Gate in a new account; Ultimate Ironman chosen on Tutorial Island.
            Carry in what the tutorial leaves you, then the Lumbridge starter kit: about 10 to 17/28.
            Exit 15 Firemaking, about 19 Woodcutting and 7 Thieving; Misthalin Mystery done and the local quests started; the Draynor kit of about 18 items; about 30 gp.
            Carries forward the air talisman (Rune Mysteries), the arrow shafts, the jug of wine supply.

            Nothing in this block is risky. If you die anywhere in Lumbridge or Draynor, you respawn in Lumbridge and your items wait on the ground where you fell for 60 minutes of logged-in time. Walk straight back and pick everything up before doing anything else.

            Set up the account on Tutorial Island

            Pick your look. The old advice to start as a female character no longer applies, so choose whatever you like. Before you leave the island, set up two-step authentication on the account. A UIM is months of work held in one inventory with no bank behind it, and a hijacked account can be emptied in a single death.

            On the island, talk to Paul, the Iron Man tutor, and choose Ultimate Ironman. Do it before the Magic instructor sends you to the mainland: once you leave the island the mode can only ever be downgraded (at Adam in Lumbridge), never chosen again. Finish the rest of the tutorial normally. Paul lets you change your choice as often as you like while you are still on the island.

            When you arrive in Lumbridge, clean the Tutorial Island load at once rather than carrying it in case it is useful. The tutorial hands you a bronze axe, tinderbox, small fishing net, shrimps, bucket, pot, bread, bronze pickaxe, bronze dagger, bronze sword, wooden shield, shortbow, bronze arrows and five rune stacks. Treat them like this:

            • Eat the shrimps and the bread.
            • Wear the bronze sword and the wooden shield. Worn items cost no inventory slots, and the pair gives some Defence until the steel axe takes the weapon slot in Varrock.
            • Keep the bronze axe (until Bob sells you a steel one), the tinderbox, the small fishing net (the Ardougne diary fish on the Fishing Platform during Sea Slug), one bucket, and the air and mind rune stacks.
            • Sell the bronze dagger, shortbow, bronze arrows, pot and bronze pickaxe to the Lumbridge General Store, and drop the water, earth and body runes. Nothing in this section uses them, and each is cheap to replace when a later step does. A UIM starts with no coins in hand (the tutorial's 25 coins go to a bank you can't open), so the few coins from this sale are all you have until the pickpocketing below (the knife is a free spawn in the castle cellar).

            Leave your respawn point in Lumbridge for the whole of Chapter 1. Every death in the chapter, planned or not, sends you back to Lumbridge, so every walk to a pile starts there.

            Done when you are in Lumbridge as an Ultimate Ironman.

            Coins: a few coins from selling the tutorial items · Slots: about 7/28 · Requirements: none

            Build the Lumbridge starter kit and reach 15 Firemaking

            Take the knife and the jug that spawn in the Lumbridge Castle cellar (down the kitchen trapdoor; both are free), and fill the jug with water at the nearest sink or fountain. The jug of water is for Brother Cedric in Monk's Friend, so it rides along until Ardougne. Pick up a pair of leather boots in the same cellar and wear them; they are your feet slot until the clue hunter boots replace them. The cellar also has a bucket and a second pair of leather boots; if you have already sold or dropped the tutorial bucket, take the spare here, because the guide needs one bucket through to Gertrude's Cat.

            Climb to the first floor of Lumbridge Castle and talk to Duke Horacio in his bedroom to start Rune Mysteries. He gives you the air talisman, which stays in your inventory until you trade it to Archmage Sedridor at the Wizards' Tower, on the island south of Draynor, later in this section.

            Train Firemaking to 15 next to any group of normal trees. Chop one log, light it where you stand, and repeat. You never hold more than one log, so the training costs one slot. 15 Firemaking is 2,411 XP, about 61 logs at 40 XP each. Don't save ashes: whenever a later quest wants some, you make them in seconds with a log and the tinderbox. The rest of your Firemaking up to 50 comes the same way, burning each log as you chop it: oaks in Ardougne, then teaks in Varlamore, so you never carry logs for it.

            If you want the arrow route to 46 Fletching in section 1.2, make your arrow shafts now while you're surrounded by normal trees. Chop a full inventory of logs, fletch them with the knife (15 shafts per log), and repeat until you have about 1,500 shafts from 100 logs. Shafts stack, so the whole batch ends up in one slot and you leave Lumbridge carrying no logs. If you would rather take your Fletching from Wintertodt kindling, skip the shafts. With the shafts, the chopping here (about 161 logs at 25 XP each) puts you at about 19 Woodcutting; without them, about 11.

            Done when you are 15 Firemaking with the starter kit.

            Coins: unchanged · Slots: about 11/28 · Requirements: none

            Fund the Lumbridge route and start the local quests

            Pickpocket the men around Lumbridge Castle until you hold about 250 gp. Each success gives 3 gp and 8 Thieving XP, so this takes about 85 successful pickpockets and leaves you near 7 Thieving, already past the 5 that Ardougne's baker's stall needs. The 250 gp pays for the spade, the hammer and the steel axe below with a small cushion. Pickpocketing a man in Lumbridge is also a Lumbridge & Draynor easy diary task, so your first success ticks it. A failed attempt stuns you for 4.8 seconds and deals 1 damage, so over 85 successes watch your Hitpoints and pause to regenerate rather than eat: you have no food to spare until the wines in Draynor.

            While you are in the courtyard, talk to Hans and ask how long you have been in Lumbridge; it is a Lumbridge diary task that costs nothing now and a detour later.

            Walk into The Sheared Ram and talk to Veos to start X Marks the Spot. He hands you a treasure scroll: keep it, it leads to four digs and each dig swaps it for the next clue, so it never takes more than one slot. If you get stuck on any of the digs, Veos gives hints here in The Sheared Ram, and again on the Port Sarim dock where the quest ends.

            Don't count on free runes from a tutor here: since the 3 November 2026 update the Lumbridge combat tutors are one NPC, Captain Harlan, and he hands out 40 air runes, 40 mind runes and a staff only after enough progress in Mudfoot's Big Adventure, at most once every 30 minutes. Keep the stacks you have.

            Buy a spade and a hammer from the Lumbridge General Store. Talk to Father Aereck in the church to start The Restless Ghost. Buy a steel axe from Bob's Brilliant Axes, then drop the bronze axe in the shop: Bob sells another one for a few coins if any later step wants a throwaway axe. The steel axe costs 200 gp, most of your 250, which is why it is the last purchase. It needs 6 Woodcutting to chop with, which your Firemaking training has already given you.

            Before you leave Bob's, step outside and stand on the tile directly north of the shop's north-western window, just west of the plant there. The scroll's directions (one east, seven north, five west and one south from the shop) lead to the same tile. Dig there with the spade. That is the first X Marks the Spot dig, and it replaces the scroll with the next clue.

            Done when the local quests are started.

            Coins: about 30 gp · Slots: about 14/28 · Requirements: none

            Complete Misthalin Mystery and gear up in Draynor

            Go south into the Lumbridge Swamp and complete Misthalin Mystery, which starts at the jetty there: talk to Abigale in the south-eastern corner of the swamp, near the fishing spots, and take the rowboat south of her to the island. It needs no items of your own (the bucket, tinderbox and knife it uses are found during the quest) and no combat. Two things can set you back. Read the notes in the order you find them, because each part opens only once the earlier note is read. During the first cutscene with the killer, click only the text box to continue: clicking elsewhere or pressing the space bar ends it early and puts you out of the room. It rewards an uncut ruby, emerald and sapphire, plus 600 Crafting XP. Mandy, outside the mansion, hands the reward over only if you have three free slots, which you have with this kit. Drop all three: the Wintertodt Reward Cart can roll the same gems noted (its gem roll is random), which is one slot per type instead of three loose stones carried through the whole section.

            Walk west through the swamp to Father Urhney's house, a small hut by the sea in the western part of the swamp. Ask him for the ghostspeak amulet; he only hands it over once The Restless Ghost has been started, which you just did. Wear it straight away. If you lose it later, Urhney gives you another when you ask; the guide relies on that in 1.3, after the rainbow scarf has taken your neck slot. Pick up the leather gloves in his house and wear them too.

            Continue west to Draynor Village. Talk to the Friendly Forester at the Forestry shop for a free forestry kit and wear it in your cape slot; from now on its Forestry items go into it instead of your inventory. The kit is also what makes Forestry events appear while you chop, which the teak session later in 1.1 and the log basket in 1.4 both depend on, so keep it on until a step replaces it. In the market, buy four jugs of wine from Fortunato. The shop sells them for 1 gp each and keeps five in stock. Four is deliberate. Wine heals 11 Hitpoints but doesn't stack, and the empty jug stays in the slot after you drink it, so it is only food for the lever trip and the Clock Tower dungeon. Each jug also lowers your Attack by 2 for a while, which doesn't matter, because you drink them only while running past monsters. From Ardougne onward you eat cakes from the stall.

            Done when Misthalin Mystery is done and you hold the Draynor kit.

            Coins: about 30 gp · Slots: about 18/28 · Requirements: none

            Wiki references Ultimate Ironman Mode Rune Mysteries X Marks the Spot The Restless Ghost Misthalin Mystery Ghostspeak amulet

            The levers to Ardougne and the three Ardougne quests

            This block moves you to Ardougne and closes Clock Tower, Monk's Friend and Hazeel Cult in a single pass, then trains Woodcutting to 35. You carry the Draynor kit with about ten free slots. That is enough because Clock Tower lets you hold only one cog at a time and every other quest item here is handed in within minutes of being picked up. If you ever run short of space, finish the next hand-in instead of dropping a quest item.

            Gate in the Draynor kit with about ten free slots.
            Carry in the Draynor kit: about 18/28.
            Exit Clock Tower, Monk's Friend and Hazeel Cult done; 35 Woodcutting; about 2,500 gp.
            Carries forward nothing beyond the Draynor kit; Monk's Friend's eight law runes are dropped as soon as the quest is done.

            The dangers are the trip through the Deserted Keep (Wilderness levels 50 to 54) and two groups of monsters you run past in the Clock Tower and Monk's Friend dungeons: level 14 thieves with a level 26 Head Thief, and level 53 ogres. Keep your four wines for those. None of them has to be fought: every step below runs past them.

            Take the levers to Ardougne

            Ardougne holds the next three quests and most of your early money, and at this point nothing teleports you there: the Ardougne Teleport spell needs Plague City and 51 Magic. The lever chain gets you there in a couple of minutes and completes an easy Ardougne Diary task on the way, which is why the guide takes it instead of the long walk west.

            Open the grouping tab and use the Clan Wars minigame teleport. It drops you at the Ferox Enclave, a safe zone inside the Wilderness where players can't attack each other. In the chapel, the two Pools of Refreshment restore your Hitpoints, Prayer and run energy, cure poison, venom and disease, and reset changed stats for free (they don't restore special attack and they switch off active prayers). Use one even if you feel fine: full run energy is what gets you away from the levers if someone is waiting there. Minigame teleports share a 20-minute cooldown, so the next one you need (Fishing Trawler, much later) will be ready long before you get there.

            Inside the enclave, take the Soul Wars portal in the dungeon at the north-eastern end, then leave the Soul Wars lobby through the exit portal to Edgeville. The Edgeville lever is in the south of the village, just east of the yew trees and south of the bank. The first pull shows a warning that you are about to enter the deep Wilderness; confirm it. You land in the Deserted Keep, an island ringed by lava that spans Wilderness levels 50 to 54. A web blocks the keep's entrance, but you arrive inside it and never leave on foot, so you don't need to cut it. Pull the keep's lever straight away. Until you complete the easy Wilderness Diary it has only one destination, the lever in East Ardougne, so there is no menu to read.

            The Ardougne lever stands in a building in the west of East Ardougne, just north of the gate between the two cities, and pulling it is one of the easy Ardougne Diary tasks. Pull it once: the task completes and you are back in the keep. Pull the keep's lever again and you end the trip in Ardougne, a few steps from where the next step starts.

            The two short stops in the keep are the only deep-Wilderness moments in Chapter 1. Do them on a quiet world, never a High Risk world (the Edgeville lever adds a second warning if you are on one), and don't pause between levers. If a player attacks you, pull the lever at once; for a UIM, a death in the Wilderness converts most of your items into coins for the killer, so there is no pile to walk back to. If someone is already standing in the keep when you arrive from Edgeville, leave by the lever as normal and skip the second pull in Ardougne: the diary task waits for any quiet visit later, because the lever never moves. If you would rather not enter the Wilderness at all, take the green Castle Wars portal just outside The Old Nite pub on the east side of Ferox instead, and walk north-east from Castle Wars to Ardougne. It takes longer and leaves the diary task for later, but nothing is at risk.

            Don't collect iron bars at Ferox or nettles in Edgeville on this trip. Iron bars arrive with the step that smiths them, and nettles grow next to the place Recipe for Disaster uses them.

            Done when you are in Ardougne.

            Coins: about 30 gp · Slots: about 18/28 · Requirements: none

            Start Hazeel Cult and Monk's Friend

            Run to the Carnillean house in the south-west of East Ardougne and talk to Ceril Carnillean to start Hazeel Cult. Walk east to the cave entrance next to the river, east of the clock tower, and talk to Clivet inside. Choose to help the cult. He gives you a bottle of poison and asks you to slip it into Ceril's food. The poison is used in a few minutes, so leave it in your inventory.

            Run south to the Ardougne Monastery and talk to Brother Omad to start Monk's Friend. He explains that thieves stole a child's blanket and you need to fetch it before he can hold a party.

            Slots: about 19/28 with the poison

            Done when both quests are started.

            Finish Clock Tower and Monk's Friend

            Walk to the clock tower south of Ardougne and talk to Brother Kojo to start Clock Tower. Four cogs, one of each colour, are hidden in the dungeon under the tower and in the tunnels around it, and each one goes on the spindle of its own colour, on a different floor of the tower's central column. The game lets you hold only one cog at a time, whatever its colour, so you can't gather them all and fit them in one climb. The order below fits each cog before fetching the next and threads the Monk's Friend errands into the walks between them, so the blanket, the jug of water and the log are each handed in minutes after you pick them up.

            • White cog. Climb down the ladder in the middle room of the tower. Take the north-western door and pick up the rat poison in the tunnel; leave any other poison where it lies, because Hooknosed Jack makes the Ratcatchers poison for you in Chapter 2. Follow the passage to its end, where two levers on the north wall of the rat cage open it: pull both up. Go through the gates and use the poison on the rats' food trough, then wait until every rat is dead. Go through the western gate, take the white cog and climb the ladder there. Carry it all the way to the top floor of the tower and use it on the white spindle, on the west side of the column.
            • Child's blanket. Leave the tower and walk to the ring of grey stones south-west of it, east of the Gnome Battlefield. Climb down the ladder inside the ring, take the first path south to the room at the end and take the child's blanket from the table. Don't fight: run straight past the two level 14 thieves and the level 26 Head Thief, and drink a wine if your Hitpoints drop. At 1 Prayer you have no protection prayers. Climb out and take the blanket to Brother Omad. Talk to him again: he can't hold the party without Brother Cedric, who was meant to bring the wine. Ask him who Cedric is and then where to find him. Skipping either question leaves the quest stuck, so take both options.
            • Brother Cedric. Cedric is just south of the Ardougne Zoo camel cage, next to a well. Give him your jug of water to sober him up; the well beside him can't fill a jug directly, which is why you filled it in Lumbridge. Chop one normal log from the trees nearby and give him that too, so he can fix his cart. Only regular logs work, so don't hand him an oak. Fill your Tutorial Island bucket at the well; a spare bucket lies beside it if you dropped yours. You now hold a bucket of water for the black cog, and you keep that bucket afterwards for Gertrude's Cat.
            • Blue cog. Use the ladder just east of Cedric and follow the tunnel to its end. Push the wall there, take the blue cog and climb out. Run back into the tower, go to the centre room and climb the staircase to the first floor, where the blue spindle is on the south side of the column.
            • Black cog. Climb down to the basement again and take the north-eastern door. Go as far east as the corridor allows, to a room with walls of fire around a cog. Use your bucket of water on the black cog and read the message through to the end before you click anything: if you close the dialogue early, the cog stays on the floor. Run back to the entrance ladder without climbing it and use the cog on the black spindle, on the east side of the basement column there. Keep the empty bucket.
            • Red cog. Take the south-eastern basement door and run past the level 53 ogres to the red cog. They hit far harder than anything you can trade blows with yet, so don't stop to fight, and drink a wine if they catch you. Run back to the ladder, climb to the ground floor and use the cog on the red spindle, on the north face of the column beside the ladder.

            Talk to Brother Kojo to complete Clock Tower for 500 gp and 1,000 Firemaking XP. Then go back to the monastery and talk to Brother Omad, who now holds his party, to complete Monk's Friend. Its reward includes 2,000 Woodcutting XP and eight law runes, and afterwards Omad throws a party whenever you talk to him. Drop the laws straight away. Nothing in this guide casts a spell with them before the Mage Training Arena in 1.3, which pays you law runes back by the hundred, and carried until then they would cost a slot through all of section 1.1.

            Done when Clock Tower and Monk's Friend are complete.

            Coins: about 530 gp · Slots: about 18/28, the bucket replacing the jug and the used wines · Requirements: none

            Finish Hazeel Cult

            Walk back to the Carnillean house, in the south-western corner of East Ardougne, and go down to the basement and use the poison on the range. The family dog, Scruffy, eats the meal instead of Ceril; Clivet accepts that as proof of your loyalty anyway. Go upstairs and talk to Ceril, then return to Clivet in the cave. He gives you Hazeel's mark and tells you to reach the hideout by redirecting the sewers.

            Turn the five sewer valves around the river south of East Ardougne in this order:

            1. Valve 1, north of the cave entrance: turn LEFT.
            2. Valve 2, at the southern end of the bridge by the Carnillean house: turn RIGHT.
            3. Valve 3, north-east of Valve 2 across the bridge: turn RIGHT.
            4. Valve 4, back across the river toward the zoo's penguin cage: turn RIGHT.
            5. Valve 5, south-east of Valve 4, past the monk: turn RIGHT.

            Enter the cave, board the raft and talk to Alomone at the hideout. If the raft lands you on the wrong island, it stops at the island matching the valve you got wrong; fix that valve and try again. If the raft doesn't leave the shore at all, go back and check every valve against the list. On Hazeel's side Alomone doesn't fight you. He asks you to help the cult's undercover butler recover a scroll hidden in the house.

            Go back to the Carnillean house and talk to Butler Jones. Search the crate in the basement and finish the dialogue so that the chest key actually lands in your inventory. Climb the ladder, then the stairs. In the east room, knock on the northern part of the wall, climb the ladder that opens behind it, and use the key on the chest to take the scroll. Return to Alomone with the scroll to complete Hazeel Cult, for 2,000 gp and 1,500 Thieving XP.

            Drop Hazeel's mark. It is a neck item, it would compete with the ghostspeak amulet and later the rainbow scarf, and nothing in this guide needs it. The mark is purely cosmetic, so dropping it costs you nothing.

            Done when Hazeel Cult is complete.

            Coins: about 2,530 gp · Slots: about 17/28 · Requirements: none

            Train to 35 Woodcutting

            Train Woodcutting next to the Carnillean house or any spot near Ardougne with normal and oak trees. Chop a normal log and burn it on the spot, over and over, until 15 Woodcutting, then switch to oaks (if the arrow shafts already took you past 15, start on oaks straight away) and keep chopping and burning each log immediately. One free slot is all this needs. If no oaks are close to the house, there are clusters of them inside East Ardougne, east of the market. An oak gives several logs before it falls, after about 27 seconds of chopping, so you don't need to change trees after every log.

            Stop at 35 Woodcutting, which is 22,406 XP. Starting from about 19 Woodcutting and with Monk's Friend's 2,000 XP already banked, that is roughly 440 oak logs at 37.5 XP each (about 500 if you skipped the arrow shafts). Burning them all at 60 XP each takes Firemaking from 15 to about 37. Both levels matter later: 35 Woodcutting unlocks teaks in Varlamore, and every Firemaking level before 50 shortens the teak session there.

            Done when you are 35 Woodcutting.

            Coins: about 2,500 gp · Slots: about 17/28 · Requirements: none

            Wiki references Ferox Enclave Ardougne Diary Clock Tower Monk's Friend Hazeel Cult Sewer valve

            Ardougne money and the first warm clothing

            This block takes your coin stack from about 2,500 to about 7,500 gp, gets you to 20+ Thieving, completes Sheep Herder and puts the first two Wintertodt warm items on your body. Before you start, drop any empty wine jugs; from here your food is cakes from the stall.

            Gate in 5 Thieving for the cake stall; 20 Thieving for silk; 100 gp for Sheep Herder.
            Carry in the Ardougne kit with five cakes, three slots kept for Sheep Herder: up to 28/28.
            Exit 20+ Thieving; Sheep Herder done; the clue hunter gloves and boots worn; about 7,500 gp.
            Carries forward two warm items for Wintertodt.

            The money comes from three sources: Sheep Herder's 3,100 gp reward, the silk stall, and the cake stall's diary task on the side. Silk is the awkward one for a UIM. It doesn't stack and you have nowhere to put it, so it is stolen and sold one inventory at a time. The silk merchant also refuses to buy from anyone he remembers stealing from him, and his memory lasts ten logged-in minutes. Sheep Herder and the clue hunter dig are scheduled into exactly that wait, so no time is spent standing around.

            Restore, check pets, and steal cakes to 20 Thieving

            Start with two free Ardougne easy diary tasks. Switch Thick Skin on for a moment so you are below full Prayer (the task only counts then), pray at the altar in the East Ardougne church to restore it, then walk to the pet shop and right-click Probita to check your insured pets. Both tasks need nothing but the walk, which is why they come first.

            Go to the baker's stall in the market and steal cakes. Your Lumbridge pickpocketing and Hazeel Cult's 1,500 Thieving XP have already put you past the 5 Thieving the stall needs, and the first successful cake completes another Ardougne easy diary task. Each steal gives 16 XP; keep going until 20 Thieving (4,470 XP). If the baker or a guard spots you mid-theft, they attack: step away a few tiles until they lose interest, then carry on. About two steals in three give a cake; the rest give bread or, less often, a chocolate slice.

            Eat cakes whenever you need to heal while you train, keep about five as food for the next blocks (each cake heals 12 Hitpoints in three bites of 4, so five cakes are 60 Hitpoints in five slots), and drop every bread and chocolate slice the stall gives you the moment it appears. This is not a food stockpile. Wintertodt heals you with its own potions, and nothing before it needs more than a few cakes.

            Done when you are 20 Thieving with five cakes.

            Coins: about 2,500 gp · Slots: about 18/28 with five cakes · Requirements: 5 Thieving for the stall

            Fill the first silk load

            At 20 Thieving, move to the silk stall and steal until only three slots are free (Sheep Herder needs them); with your kit and five cakes, that is about seven pieces. To make the load worth the wait, drop the cakes for this load only and steal another five, which takes you to about twelve pieces. The stall gives 24 Thieving XP per steal. Ardougne Market has two silk stalls. The south-west one has fewer guards around it, and the owner of the north-east one isn't nearby. Guards and Knights of Ardougne can catch you; the hero and the paladin in the market do nothing unless your combat level is near theirs.

            Then leave the stall alone completely. From your last theft, the silk merchant remembers you for 1,000 consecutive logged-in ticks, which is ten minutes. Logging out pauses the count, so stay logged in. Stealing from the baker's stall doesn't reset his memory; only the silk stalls do. Every theft from a silk stall, including the one in Kourend Castle's courtyard, starts the count again from zero, so finish a load before you begin the wait.

            Slots: 25/28, three kept free for Sheep Herder · Requirements: 20 Thieving

            Done when the first silk load is in your inventory.

            Complete Sheep Herder during the first wait

            Walk to East Ardougne church and talk to Councillor Halgrive outside it, in the graveyard just south of the church, to start Sheep Herder, with at least three free slots: the quest hands you the poisoned sheep feed, and Doctor Orbon the plague jacket and trousers. Then talk to Doctor Orbon inside the church and pay him 100 gp for the plague jacket and plague trousers. Wear both; worn items take no slots, so the silk can stay in your inventory. The wiki says the plague jacket has gloves built in and takes yours off, so they land in your bag: drop the leather gloves, because the clue hunter gloves replace them in the next step and you need that slot for the cattleprod's swap and the sheep bones. Sheep Herder gives 4 quest points.

            Walk to the enclosure north-west of the Ardougne north bank. Go in through its west gate, pick up the cattleprod from the ground a few steps south of the incinerator and wield it, then leave by the same gate. The four plague sheep stand outside the pen: blue to the north-west, yellow to the north, green to the east and red to the south of the incinerator. The red one is just north of the north bank, the green one north-east of it, the blue one near a camp of warriors, and the yellow one south of the Fishing Guild. Level 42 moss giants roam near the blue sheep, so keep your distance from them. Prod one sheep of each colour through the gate, pushing from the side away from the pen. A sheep that bleats loudly ("BAAAA") is going where you push it, one that answers with a quiet "Baa" is heading back, and a sheep left alone too long walks back to where it started. Once a sheep reaches the gate it goes in by itself. Then use the sheep feed on them until all four die. Pick up each set of bones and use them on the incinerator. Report back to Councillor Halgrive to complete the quest. It pays 3,100 gp (3,000 gp plus your 100 gp for the plague suit back), which is most of this block's money, plus 1,000 Hunter XP.

            Drop the plague jacket, plague trousers, cattleprod and any leftover feed the moment the quest completes. None of them is used again.

            Done when Sheep Herder is complete.

            Coins: about 5,500 gp · Slots: 28/28, silk still on you · Requirements: 100 gp

            Dig up the clue hunter gloves and boots

            On your way back south, stop at the oak tree south-west of the Fishing Guild, just north of East Ardougne, and dig next to it with your spade. This is the dig from the Week 1 Crack the Clue map, and it needs nothing but the spade. You get the clue hunter gloves and clue hunter boots. Wear both and drop the leather gloves and leather boots they replace. These are warm items one and two for Wintertodt, and from now on they stay in your gloves and feet slots.

            If your inventory is completely full of silk, drop one piece before digging, then put the gloves and boots on immediately; they never need a free slot for longer than a click. If a piece is ever destroyed, dig at the same oak again to get it back.

            Slots: about 25/28 · Requirements: none

            Done when you wear the clue hunter gloves and boots.

            Sell the silk and run the remaining loads

            By the time you're back in the market, the ten minutes have passed. Talk to the silk merchant and choose to sell. Offer 120 gp per piece; he counters with 50. Answer with 60 and he buys every piece of silk in your inventory at once. The first 60 gp sale completes another Ardougne easy diary task. Keep to that order. Other answers end the haggle at 20, 40 or 50 gp a piece, and only a sale at 60 gp counts for the diary.

            Steal and sell two more loads the same way, and fill its ten-minute wait with whatever you like near the market: a few more cake steals, or walking to the docks to check the boat. Steal a new load only after the current one is sold, so the inventory never overflows. Three loads of about twelve silk plus Sheep Herder put you at about 7,500 gp, which covers everything up to the end of the section. If you want a bigger cushion, run a fourth.

            Keep no silk afterwards. Dragon Slayer I and Ghosts Ahoy each need one piece much later, and the Al Kharid silk trader sells it for 3 gp when you are there. Steal five cakes from the baker's stall again before leaving.

            Done when you hold about 7,500 gp.

            Coins: about 7,500 gp · Slots: about 18/28 with five cakes · Thieving: about 21

            Wiki references Ardougne Diary Silk merchant Silk theft timer Sheep Herder Clue hunter outfit

            Brimhaven and Witchaven: Tribal Totem and Sea Slug

            This block completes Sea Slug and collects the tribal totem, and you leave it holding exactly one new long-term item: a raw sardine for Gertrude's Cat. You need about six free slots at any moment here, which your five cakes and the Ardougne kit leave you.

            Gate in 30 Firemaking for Sea Slug; 21 Thieving for the mansion stairs; about six free slots.
            Carry in the Ardougne kit and five cakes: about 18/28.
            Exit Sea Slug done; the tribal totem collected; a raw sardine for Gertrude's Cat; about 7,500 gp.
            Carries forward the totem (Tribal Totem), the sardine (Gertrude's Cat).

            Don't stock up on this trip on soda ash, buckets of sand, a cake tin, a glassblowing pipe, two fishing rods, a fly fishing rod, a harpoon, bait or a lobster pot. Each item is sold next to the place that uses it, and carrying them would cost you a dozen slots through Daddy's Home and Wintertodt for nothing.

            Start Tribal Totem in Brimhaven

            Walk to the Ardougne docks and take Captain Barnaby's boat to Brimhaven for 30 gp. On the Brimhaven docks, buy one swamp paste from the Trader Stan's crew member. That single swamp paste is for Sea Slug; Holgart takes it from you within the hour. Check the name before you pay: Holgart wants swamp paste, not swamp tar, and the tar does nothing for his boat.

            Walk north into the Shrimp and Parrot and talk to Kangai Mau to start Tribal Totem. He wants his tribe's totem back from Lord Handelmort's mansion in Ardougne. Tell him you are in search of adventure, then agree to get it back. Take the boat straight back to Ardougne (30 gp).

            Done when Tribal Totem is started.

            Coins: about 7,400 gp · Slots: about 19/28 · Requirements: none to start

            Complete Sea Slug and fish for the diary

            Walk east from Ardougne to Witchaven. Sea Slug needs 30 Firemaking, which your oak burning has already given you. Talk to Caroline on the coast just north of the village to start Sea Slug. Holgart stands next to her: give him the swamp paste so that he can repair his boat and row you out to the fishing platform. From then on you can right-click him and choose Travel.

            On the platform, talk to Bailey in the small room in the south-west corner and pick up the broken glass from its floor, then the damp sticks in the north-east corner. Don't pick up any of the sea slugs on the floor, because each one hurts you for 3 damage. Climb the ladder beside the sticks, enter the west cabin, past the crane, and talk to Kennith, Caroline's son, who won't come out. Climb down, take Holgart's boat to the small island to talk to Kennith's father Kent, and ride back. Talk to Bailey again for an unlit torch. Use the broken glass on the damp sticks to dry them, then rub the dry sticks together to light the unlit torch. Light it before you climb the ladder a second time: going up with an unlit torch lets a fisherman hit you on the way. Climb up, talk to Kennith, kick the badly repaired wall from outside his cabin, use the lit torch on a nearby fisherman or sea slug, talk to him again, then rotate the crane outside the cabin to lower him to the boat. Ride back with Holgart and talk to Caroline to complete the quest. The reward is 7,175 Fishing XP and oyster pearls. Sea Slug is also a requirement for The Slug Menace, the quest behind the proselyte armour that Chapter 2 buys.

            While you're still on the Fishing Platform, catch any fish there with your small fishing net for the Ardougne medium diary (a catch on the Witchaven shore doesn't count), then drop the fish and the net; you won't fish again until Catherby in 1.2. The platform's six small-net spots are all on the far east side of its ground floor, so do this before you ask Holgart for the last ride. Keep the oyster pearls and sell them at the Ardougne General Store on your way through; they are worth a little and cost slots if you hold them.

            Before you leave Witchaven, step into Lovecraft's Tackle and buy one raw sardine. It costs 10 gp. It rides along until the Lumber Yard in Varrock, where Gertrude's cat eats it once seasoned. It is the only item this section carries that far on purpose.

            Done when Sea Slug is complete.

            Coins: about 7,400 gp · Slots: about 19/28 · Requirements: 30 Firemaking

            Collect the totem from Handelmort Mansion

            Back in East Ardougne, go to the G.P.D.T. depot south of the eastern bank. Investigate the north-easternmost crate to take its address label, which is addressed to Lord Handelmort's mansion. Use that label on the crate two tiles south of it, which holds Wizard Cromperty's experimental teleport block, then ask a G.P.D.T. employee when the crate will be delivered. Walk to Wizard Cromperty's house north-east of the market and ask him to teleport you: ask what he has invented, ask to be teleported and confirm. You land next to his block, which is now inside the mansion.

            Go west two doors and open the combination lock with the code K-U-R-T; if the game says the combination is incorrect, enter it more slowly. The lock is set with arrow buttons, one letter at a time, and clicking too quickly is what makes a correct code fail. Investigate the stairs before you climb them. That needs 21 Thieving, which the silk stall has only just given you: three loads put you at about 5,260 XP, barely past 21 (5,018). If you're short, steal a few more cakes or silk first. If investigating turns up nothing, your level is too low; don't use Climb-up anyway. The stairs are trapped, and setting the trap off drops you into the Ardougne Sewers. If that happens, walk south-west through the sewer, climb the ladder out and ask Cromperty for another teleport; you won't need the door code a second time. Climb up, go out of the south door onto the balcony and into the room to the east, search the chest there and finish its dialogue to take the totem.

            Walk out of the mansion and back to the Ardougne docks. The totem goes to Kangai Mau at the start of the next block.

            Done when you hold the totem and the sardine.

            Coins: about 7,500 gp after selling the pearls · Slots: about 19/28 with the sardine and totem · Requirements: 21 Thieving for the stairs

            Wiki references Tribal Totem Sea Slug Lovecraft's Tackle Ardougne Diary

            The Lumbridge loop

            This loop closes Tribal Totem, Sheep Shearer and The Restless Ghost, brings X Marks the Spot to its last stop, and gives you the Chronicle and your third warm item. You start it holding the totem and the sardine on top of the Ardougne kit, and you finish it holding only two items you didn't have before: the Chronicle and the research package.

            Gate in the totem and the sardine on top of the Ardougne kit.
            Carry in the kit, totem and sardine: about 19/28.
            Exit Tribal Totem, Sheep Shearer and The Restless Ghost done; X Marks the Spot at its last stop; the Chronicle; the third warm item; about 6,510 gp.
            Carries forward the casket and onion, the empty bucket, the sardine.

            The order matters for the inventory. The wool is spun and handed in ten balls at a time, the ghost's skull goes into the coffin within a few minutes of being picked up, and each X Marks the Spot dig swaps the clue for the next one. Fred takes the wool in any amount and keeps count, so you hand it in in batches: with the cakes dropped for the swordfish you have about nine free slots, room for the shears and eight wool at a time.

            Complete Tribal Totem and return to Lumbridge

            Take the boat from Ardougne to Brimhaven (30 gp) and give the totem to Kangai Mau in the Shrimp and Parrot to complete Tribal Totem. The reward is 1,775 Thieving XP and five swordfish. Keep the swordfish as your food from here to Varrock and drop your remaining cakes now: Sheep Shearer needs the slots.

            Cast Lumbridge Home Teleport from the standard spellbook. It has a 30-minute cooldown, so note the time: if it is ready again when you finish at the Wizards' Tower, you can use it a second time for the return to the graveyard.

            Done when Tribal Totem is complete.

            Coins: about 7,470 gp · Slots: about 18/28 · Requirements: none

            Talk to the ghost and make the castle dig

            With the ghostspeak amulet still on, walk to the small building in the Lumbridge graveyard, open the coffin and talk to the ghost for The Restless Ghost. The building stands in the south-east corner of the graveyard, next to the chapel where Father Aereck started the quest. He tells you that his skull was stolen and is at the Wizards' Tower. Without the amulet you would only hear "wooo", which is why it has stayed in your neck slot until now. If you have lost the amulet, Father Urhney in his house on the west side of the Lumbridge Swamp gives you another.

            Walk round to the back exit of Lumbridge Castle, next to the kitchen. Stand one tile south-west of the large crate outside the door and dig. That is the second X Marks the Spot dig; it swaps your clue for a mysterious orb that points you to Draynor.

            Slots: about 18/28 · Requirements: ghostspeak amulet worn

            Done when the ghost is spoken to and the castle dig is done.

            Complete Sheep Shearer in two halves

            Run north to Fred the Farmer's house and talk to him to start Sheep Shearer. He hands you a pair of shears if you don't have any. Enter the sheep pen on the east side of his house and shear as many sheep as your free slots allow (about nine); skinny sheep have already been shorn, so click the woolly ones. Watch for rams, the horned ones: they can't be shorn and a misclick attacks them, so set your attack option to hidden if you keep hitting them. A sheep sometimes runs off as you shear; keep clicking, and a shorn sheep grows its wool back after a minute.

            Carry the wool to Lumbridge Castle and climb to the spinning wheel on the first floor (second floor US). It is in the bedroom at the south end of that floor. Spin them into balls of wool, walk back to Fred and hand them over. Fred accepts balls of wool in any amount and keeps count, so shear as many as your free slots allow (about nine), and repeat until he has all twenty, which completes the quest, for 150 Crafting XP and 60 gp. Spinning the wool yourself also gives about 50 Crafting XP on top of the reward, and Fred won't take noted balls in any case.

            Drop the shears; Fred gives you another pair if a later quest needs one. Before you leave the farm, pick one onion from the onion patch nearby. It is for Witch's Potion in Rimmington, which you finish in the next block. Picking it here costs one slot for about an hour, which is cheaper than the walk to the onion field north of Rimmington later.

            Done when Sheep Shearer is complete.

            Coins: about 7,530 gp · Slots: about 19/28 with the onion · Requirements: none

            Make the Draynor digs and visit Diango

            Run west to Draynor Village with the orb. It works like a hot-and-cold clue, warming as you get closer. The dig spot is north-west of the Draynor jail, south of the wheat field near Leela: stand four tiles north of the bush there and dig for the next clue. The jail guards are aggressive to low-level players, so keep your distance from the jail itself. That clue is a cipher, ESBZOPS QJH QFO, which decodes to DRAYNOR PIG PEN. Walk to the pig pen just north of the market, next to Martin the Master Gardener, stand in the centre and dig for the ancient casket. The casket goes to Veos in Port Sarim in the next block.

            In Draynor market, visit Diango's toy shop. Buy the Chronicle for 300 gp and four teleport cards for 150 gp each, and use the cards on the Chronicle so they stop taking slots. Each teleport uses one charge, and four covers every Chronicle teleport until you pass Diango again in 1.3. The Chronicle teleports you to the Champions' Guild just south-west of Varrock, which saves you the long walk in the Varrock block. It doesn't work in the Wilderness, and Diango won't sell you a second one while you own one. Buy a rainbow scarf too (120 gp; Gilbert, south-east of Barbarian Village, gives one free). It counts as warm clothing for Wintertodt, but it is always destroyed on a death, so don't carry it into a fight you might lose.

            UIM risk If a death destroys it, buy it back from him: it comes back with the charges it had.

            Wear the rainbow scarf. It is warm item three of four for Wintertodt. It needs the neck slot, so take off the ghostspeak amulet and hold it until The Restless Ghost is finished below, then drop it. Father Urhney gives a new one for free when Nature Spirit needs it in 1.3.

            Done when the Draynor digs are done and you hold the Chronicle.

            Coins: about 6,510 gp · Slots: about 21/28 · Requirements: none

            Collect the research package and the skull

            Walk south from Draynor across the bridge to the Wizards' Tower and climb down to the basement. The ladder is in the room beside the tower's entrance. The ground and first floors are a multi-combat area with level 9 wizards, so don't stop to fight on the way in; the basement itself is not multi-combat. Talk to Archmage Sedridor and give him the air talisman for Rune Mysteries; he hands you a research package in exchange. Keep it. Aubury takes it in Varrock and gives you research notes back in the same slot, and those notes stay with you until Rune Mysteries is finished at this tower in section 1.3. It is the one deliberate long carry in this section, and it saves you a whole trip to the tower. The package can't be dropped, only destroyed, and Sedridor gives you a new one if you lose it, including after a death, so the worst case is a walk back here.

            Still in the basement, put the ghostspeak amulet back on and take the ghost's skull from the altar, across the room from the ladder. A skeleton appears as soon as you pick it up. It is only level 13, but don't fight it; climb straight back up the ladder and out of the tower, and it stops following you.

            Slots: about 22/28 · Requirements: none

            Done when you hold the research package and the skull.

            Complete The Restless Ghost

            If Home Teleport is off cooldown, cast it; otherwise run back east through Draynor to Lumbridge, a two-minute run. Walk into the graveyard building and use the skull on the coffin to complete The Restless Ghost, for 1,125 Prayer XP. It is one of the requirements for Nature Spirit, so finishing it here keeps 1.3's Morytania trip open. Take off the ghostspeak amulet, put the rainbow scarf back on, and drop the amulet.

            You leave Lumbridge with the kit, the Chronicle, the research package, the ancient casket, the onion, the raw sardine, the empty bucket and your food. Everything else from this loop has already been handed in.

            Done when The Restless Ghost is complete.

            Coins: about 6,510 gp · Slots: about 20/28 · Requirements: none

            Wiki references Tribal Totem The Restless Ghost Sheep Shearer X Marks the Spot Chronicle Rainbow scarf Rune Mysteries

            Port Sarim, Karamja, Rimmington and Falador

            This block completes X Marks the Spot and Witch's Potion, takes Pirate's Treasure to its last stage and starts two quests that continue in Varrock. You arrive with the casket, onion, sardine and empty bucket on top of the kit and the Chronicle. On Karamja the banana crate is filled one banana at a time, so a single free slot is enough there.

            Gate in the casket, onion, sardine and empty bucket; one free slot for the bananas.
            Carry in the kit and the Chronicle: about 20/28.
            Exit X Marks the Spot and Witch's Potion done; Pirate's Treasure at its last stage; A Porcine of Interest and The Knight's Sword started; about 6,640 gp.
            Carries forward the Pirate's Treasure key.

            Every Witch's Potion ingredient is bought or caught in this block and handed in within the hour. Don't buy a basket of cooking ingredients, sixteen feather packs or Sarah's full farming tool set here, and don't start The Corsair Curse or Black Knights' Fortress; all of that waits until the section that uses it.

            Start A Porcine of Interest and finish X Marks the Spot

            On your way out of Draynor, read the notice board in the village to start A Porcine of Interest. The board stands behind Fortunato's Wine Shop. The quest continues with Sarah at the Falador farm at the end of this block, so starting it here saves a walk later. Its later stages need a rope and a fight with a level 37 sourhog, so leave them for the Draynor block later in this chapter that finishes it.

            Walk south to Port Sarim and find Veos on the northernmost dock, outside the Rusty Anchor. Give him the ancient casket to complete X Marks the Spot, for 200 gp, a quest point, an antique lamp worth 300 XP and a beginner scroll box. Destroy the antique lamp: Veos keeps destroyed quest lamps and hands them back when you ask, so it waits with him at no slot cost until 1.3 spends it on Herblore. Drop the scroll box unless you want to do a beginner clue now.

            Done when X Marks the Spot is complete.

            Coins: about 6,710 gp · Slots: about 19/28 · Requirements: none

            Get the rum for Pirate's Treasure

            Talk to Redbeard Frank on the Port Sarim docks to start Pirate's Treasure. He wants Karamjan rum, which the customs officers confiscate if they find it on you when you sail back, so the rum has to be smuggled. He stands in front of the same northern dock as Veos, outside the Rusty Anchor.

            Take the boat from the Port Sarim docks to Musa Point on Karamja (30 gp). The sailors for Karamja are on the south docks; the monks there sail to Entrana instead, so don't board with them. Walk into the Karamja Spirits bar and buy one Karamjan rum from Zembo (30 gp). From this moment, don't teleport until Frank has the rum: any teleport breaks the bottle, and that includes a random event that moves you. Then walk to the banana plantation and talk to Luthas, asking him for a job. He works from the hut on the east side of the plantation. Put the rum into the crate outside his shed first, so it is hidden underneath, then fill the crate with ten bananas from the plantation trees. The crate sits outside the south-east corner of the hut. If the game answers "Why would I want to do that?", you haven't asked Luthas for the job yet. Pick a banana and use it on the crate, ten times over: the crate takes them one at a time, so one free slot is all you need. Tell Luthas the crate is full; he pays you 30 gp and ships it to Wydin's Food Store in Port Sarim.

            Sail back to Port Sarim (30 gp). The customs officer east of the plantation takes you. Walk into Gerrant's Fishy Business, take the white apron from the wall and wear it. It hangs on the back wall of the shop. Go to Wydin's Food Store next door and ask Wydin for a job: with the apron on, he lets you into the back room. Search the crate marked with bananas and take your rum. Take the apron off and drop it. Give the rum to Redbeard Frank for a chest key; the chest is upstairs in the Blue Moon Inn in Varrock, which you open in the next block.

            Done when you hold the rum and the key.

            Coins: about 6,650 gp · Slots: about 19/28 with the key · Requirements: none

            Collect the Witch's Potion ingredients and finish it in Rimmington

            While you're in Wydin's, buy one raw beef. It costs 1 gp, and he stocks only one at a time; if another player has just bought it, wait a minute for the shop to restock. Walk into Betty's magic shop and buy one eye of newt. That costs 3 gp. You already have the onion from Fred's farm.

            Walk west to Rimmington and talk to Hetty in her house to start Witch's Potion. Her house is in the south-east of the village. She wants an onion, burnt meat, an eye of newt and a rat's tail. Cook the beef on the range in the house next to the Rimmington general store, and cook the result again until it burns. Kill a rat near Hetty's house and pick up its tail. The small rats in the archery shop just west of her house drop it, but only after you have started the quest, which is why you talk to Hetty first; giant rats never drop one. Give Hetty all four ingredients, then drink from her cauldron to complete the quest, for 325 Magic XP.

            Doing it now means every ingredient lives in your inventory for well under an hour.

            Done when Witch's Potion is complete.

            Coins: about 6,640 gp · Slots: about 19/28 · Requirements: none

            Progress A Porcine of Interest and start The Knight's Sword

            Walk north to Sarah's farm shop, south of Falador, and talk to Sarah for A Porcine of Interest. While you're there, open her shop with Trade and close it without buying anything: browsing Sarah's farm shop is a task in the easy Falador diary, which Chapter 2 claims. Next, use your empty bucket on the dairy cow in the farm's pen to fill it with milk. The bucket of milk is for Gertrude's cat in the Lumber Yard. The farm has only the one dairy cow, so if another player is milking it, wait a moment rather than walking to Lumbridge for another.

            The tool leprechaun next to the Falador allotment patch here is the first storage you'll use in 1.3, when farming starts. Nothing in this section farms, so don't buy Sarah's rake, seed dibber, secateurs, trowel or watering can yet; in 1.3 you buy them and hand them straight to the leprechaun.

            Walk north into Falador and talk to the Squire in the White Knights' Castle courtyard to start The Knight's Sword. Offer to make him a new sword; that is the option that leads him to Reldo. He sends you to Reldo in Varrock, which is where the next block begins. Use the Chronicle to teleport there.

            Done when The Knight's Sword is started.

            Coins: about 6,640 gp · Slots: about 19/28 · Requirements: none

            Wiki references A Porcine of Interest X Marks the Spot Pirate's Treasure Witch's Potion The Knight's Sword Tool Leprechaun

            Varrock: quest starts, 5 Attack, Aubury and Old Man Yarlo

            Varrock is a talking block. Almost every stop is a dialogue that starts or advances a quest you continue later in this section, and the only items you gain are the saw, the seasoned sardine, one beer and three waxwood logs. The research package goes to Aubury and comes back as research notes in the same slot. The Chronicle's teleport lands you at the Champions' Guild, just south-west of the city, so run west to east and finish at Old Man Yarlo's house next to the east gate, where the next block starts.

            Gate in the research package; The Knight's Sword started with the Squire in Falador (end of the previous block); 5 Attack (trained here) for the steel axe.
            Carry in the kit: about 19/28.
            Exit the Varrock quest starts made; the steel axe wielded; the saw, seasoned sardine, a beer and three waxwood logs held; about 6,540 gp.
            Carries forward the waxwood logs (Daddy's Home) and the beer (Tower of Life's builder's shirt).

            Start Gertrude's Cat and the square quests

            Teleport with the Chronicle and run west to Gertrude's house to start Gertrude's Cat. Her cat Fluffs has gone missing, and her sons know where. Pick a few doogle leaves from the bushes outside her house, use one on your raw sardine to make a seasoned sardine, and drop the spare leaves. The bushes grow just south of the house.

            Run into Varrock square. Talk to Gertrude's sons, Shilop and Wilough, and pay them the small coin fee they ask; they tell you Fluffs is hiding in the Lumber Yard north-east of the city. The fee is 100 gp. In the same square, talk to Romeo to start Romeo & Juliet; he wants a message carried to Juliet, which you do on your way back through the west of the city in the Lumber Yard block.

            Still in the square, talk to Noah to start Children of the Sun and follow the quest through to completion; it takes a few minutes and needs no items. It is the quest that opens travel to Varlamore, so the quetzal you take in the next block depends on it, and the later Varlamore quests in this chapter need it as well. Noah and Alina stand on the east side of the square, behind Eliza's stall. The main part is tailing a guard into south-east Varrock while he keeps turning round: stay behind buildings, because if he spots you, you have to talk to Noah and start the tailing again. Afterwards you mark four disguised bandits in and around the square for Sergeant Tobyn, who stands on its north side, using the Mark option on each. Prince Itzla then joins you to question one of them, gives you leave to visit Varlamore and tells you to see Regulus Cento. Go back to Tobyn in the cell room to complete the quest.

            Done when the Varrock square quests are started.

            Coins: about 6,540 gp · Slots: about 19/28 · Requirements: none

            Advance The Knight's Sword, start Priest in Peril and Daddy's Home

            Walk north into Varrock Palace. In the library on the ground floor, talk to Reldo for The Knight's Sword (the Squire in the White Knights' Castle, at the end of the previous block, started it and sent you here). The library is the northernmost room on that floor. He tells you about the Imcando dwarves and sends you to Thurgo south of Port Sarim, whom you visit in section 1.2 together with the Falador park dig for Pirate's Treasure. Thurgo wants a redberry pie before he helps, which is why 1.2 bakes one before that visit.

            On the same floor, in the south-east of the palace, talk to King Roald to start Priest in Peril. It continues at the Paterdomus temple on the way into Morytania in 1.3, so this is a single line of dialogue now. His room is the south-easternmost one, near the main entrance.

            Leave the palace and walk to the estate agent's house in north-east Varrock. Talk to Marlo to start Daddy's Home; he asks you to fix up his father's house. Pick up the saw lying on the floor in the same building. That saw is used for Daddy's Home, your first furniture and Tower of Life, so it stays with you to the end of the section.

            Slots: about 20/28 with the saw · Requirements: none

            Done when Priest in Peril and Daddy's Home are started.

            Train 5 Attack and wield the steel axe

            Train Attack from 1 to 5 on anything that can't hurt you, such as a training dummy, a chicken or a low-level goblin, using the accurate style on your bronze sword. 5 Attack is 388 XP and takes a few minutes.

            At 5 Attack, wield the steel axe. From now on the axe lives in your weapon slot and never takes an inventory space again, which matters most at Wintertodt, where every free slot is a root you can carry. Drop the bronze sword. Keep the wooden shield on; the steel axe is one-handed.

            Slots: about 19/28 · Requirements: 5 Attack to wield the steel axe

            Done when you are 5 Attack with the steel axe wielded.

            Visit Aubury, the Blue Moon Inn and Dimintheis

            Walk to Aubury's Rune Shop in south-east Varrock and give Aubury the research package. The shop is just south of the east bank. Keep talking until he offers you a cup of tea and accept it: it fully restores your run energy. He gives you the research notes for Sedridor. They take the package's old slot and stay with you until section 1.3. They go back to Sedridor in 1.3, on the same trip that buys the Chronicle back from Diango, and that hand-in completes Rune Mysteries and frees the slot.

            UIM risk Like the package, the notes can only be destroyed, not dropped, and Aubury hands out new ones if they are lost.

            Don't buy a stack of mind, air, water, earth and fire runes here. Nothing in this section casts a combat spell, and section 1.2 buys runes from Betty in Port Sarim, one stack at a time.

            Walk to the Blue Moon Inn, south of the square. It stands just north of the city's south gate. Climb the stairs and open the chest at the far end of the hall with Redbeard Frank's key. Inside is a message saying the treasure is buried in Falador park. Read it and drop it; the dig happens in 1.2 and you won't need the note to find the spot. Buy one beer at the bar on your way out. It is for Tower of Life, where the quest takes it from you.

            Walk east to Varrock East and talk to Dimintheis in his house to start Family Crest. His house is in south-east Varrock, inside the fenced area, next to the clothes shop. Chapter 2 does the rest with his three sons, so this is another dialogue-only stop. The quest needs 40 Mining, Smithing and Crafting, 59 Magic and a fight with a level 170 demon, none of which you have yet; starting it now only saves the walk.

            Done when Aubury has the package and Dimintheis is spoken to.

            Coins: about 6,540 gp · Slots: about 19/28 · Requirements: none

            Prepare Old Man Yarlo's house

            Walk to Old Man Yarlo's house in south-east Varrock, south-west of Aubury's shop, and talk to him for Daddy's Home. Inside, remove the old broken furniture the quest marks for removal, then talk to Yarlo again. The carpet only shows its remove option on a right-click. The quest plays short cutscenes here; if you click away from one, you have to watch it again. Search the crates in the north-western corner of his kitchen for three waxwood logs. Keep them: the sawmill operator turns them into waxwood planks for free in the next block. The waxwood bed can't be built without those planks, so don't leave the house without the logs.

            Done when you hold the waxwood logs and beer.
