            What this block deliberately skips. Don't join a gang for Shield of Arrav, collect two of the three Demon Slayer keys or buy Dr Harlow's beer for Vampyre Slayer in Varrock. The half shield, the keys and the stake would each sit in your inventory for thirty or more steps. Shield of Arrav is done in a single sitting with a partner when you're ready, the Demon Slayer keys are collected in the visit that finishes the quest in 1.3, and Vampyre Slayer's beer is bought on the day you kill Count Draynor.

            Coins: about 6,540 gp · Slots: about 22/28 with the waxwood logs and beer

            Wiki references Chronicle Gertrude's Cat Romeo & Juliet Children of the Sun The Knight's Sword Priest in Peril Daddy's Home Rune Mysteries Family Crest

            Varlamore teaks, the Lumber Yard, Daddy's Home and your first Construction

            This block takes Firemaking from about 37 to 50, finishes Gertrude's Cat and Daddy's Home, and gives you a house and 10 Construction for Tower of Life. It is also the tightest moment of the section: Daddy's Home needs 18 unstackable materials in your inventory at once. This block makes room in two ways. It drops the knife and tinderbox after the teaks, because Wintertodt's crates hand out free ones. And it builds at Old Man Yarlo's before completing Gertrude's Cat, whose rewards would otherwise land in the same slots.

            Gate in Children of the Sun; 35 Woodcutting for teaks; a hammer and saw; 28 free slots for the Daddy's Home materials.
            Carry in the kit, then Daddy's Home's 18 unstackable materials: 27/28 at the peak.
            Exit 50 Firemaking; Daddy's Home and Gertrude's Cat done; a house and 10 Construction; about 2,100 gp.
            Carries forward Romeo & Juliet in progress.

            Complete the Lily Pad quest

            Run out of Varrock's east gate to Regulus Cento and ask him to take you to Varlamore by quetzal; Children of the Sun, which you finished in the square, is what unlocked it. He stands just outside the gate. You land at Civitas illa Fortis. Travel south and start and complete The Ribbiting Tale of a Lily Pad Labour Dispute. It is a short quest that needs 15 Woodcutting and nothing you aren't already carrying, and it gives 2,000 Woodcutting XP.

            It starts with Marcellus, by the hardwood tree patch at the Locus Oasis; the blue frogs you deal with sit just north of the teak grove in the middle of the oasis. The quest needs an axe, and your steel axe does. When the frogs ask you to sabotage a lily pad, chop the orange tree first to distract Dave and Jane, and don't let either of them see you at the pad. Near the end the frog Cuthbert comes back as a level 1 enemy; you have to defeat him to finish, and he can still hit you. The quest also unlocks a hardwood patch at the oasis, which you have no use for yet.

            Slots: about 22/28 · Requirements: Children of the Sun

            Done when the Lily Pad quest is complete.

            Burn teaks to 50 Firemaking

            Hop to a Forestry world (444 is the usual one) and chop teak logs at the teak trees near the quest area, lighting each log on the spot. Teaks need 35 Woodcutting, which you reached in Ardougne. Each teak gives 105 Firemaking XP and 85 Woodcutting XP, and Forestry events add bonus Woodcutting XP and anima-infused bark, which goes straight into your worn forestry kit. The six teak trees just east of the Locus Oasis are the main group here, and they are close to the quetzal landing. Events can spawn on any world, but on the dedicated worlds more players share them, and world 444 has a friends chat where players call events out. Events only spawn when someone chopping there has a forestry kit, which is one more reason to keep yours on.

            Going from about 37 to 50 Firemaking is about 71,000 XP, roughly 680 teaks. The same chopping takes Woodcutting from 35 to about 47. That number matters: from 46 Woodcutting, a steel axe already chops bruma roots at the maximum speed, so you never need a better axe for Wintertodt.

            At 50 Firemaking, drop the knife and the tinderbox. Nothing between here and Wintertodt cuts or lights anything, and the Wintertodt crates give you both for free as long as you arrive without them. Those two slots are what make Daddy's Home fit.

            Slots: about 20/28 · Requirements: 35 Woodcutting for teaks

            Done when you are 50 Firemaking.

            Find Fluffs in the Lumber Yard

            Take the quetzal back to Varrock and walk to the Lumber Yard north-east of the city. Squeeze in through the broken fence and climb the ladder to Fluffs. The broken fence is on the yard's south side, and the ladder stands in the middle of the yard; Fluffs is on the first floor. Give her the bucket of milk, then the seasoned sardine. She still won't leave, because her kittens are missing: search the crates on the ground floor of the yard until the kitten lands in your inventory, then climb back up and use it on Fluffs. Check the crates that show a yellow "Mew!": the sound moves from crate to crate, but the kitten stays in one, so keep trying different crates rather than the same one. If the search says you found it but no kitten appears, log out and back in. You would then have to search the crates again to find it. Once it's back with her, Fluffs runs home to Gertrude. Drop the empty bucket; nothing else in this section needs one.

            UIM risk Don't drop the kitten during the quest, or it runs away.

            Slots: about 13/28 plus any swordfish left: coins, the air and mind runes, the arrow shafts if you made them, spade, hammer, saw, research notes, beer, three waxwood logs and the Chronicle · Requirements: none

            Done when Fluffs is found.

            Buy the Daddy's Home materials at the sawmill

            Chop ten normal logs from the trees around the Lumber Yard. Give them to the sawmill operator to make ten planks, at 100 gp each. He stands along the yard's south wall. Give him the three waxwood logs too: because he knows Old Man Yarlo, he turns them into waxwood planks for free. From the same operator, buy five bolts of cloth at 650 gp each and a stack of about a hundred steel nails. Daddy's Home needs at least 16 nails, and some bend while you build, so the spare ones are cheap insurance. The nails cost 3 gp each. Use normal logs only: the quest's furniture takes plain planks, and oak, teak or mahogany planks won't work.

            You now hold 10 planks, 3 waxwood planks and 5 bolts of cloth on top of the nails, saw, hammer, spade, Chronicle, research notes, beer, coins, the air and mind rune stacks and, if you made them, the arrow-shaft stack. With the shafts that is 29 items, one more than an inventory holds, so make room before you buy. Drop the mind rune stack: nothing before Wintertodt casts a spell, and Betty sells mind runes when 1.2 needs them. Then wear the Chronicle. It goes in the shield slot, so drop the wooden shield it replaces: the Chronicle has no combat bonuses but still teleports while worn, and the shield's few Defence points don't matter before Wintertodt. From here the Chronicle stays worn whenever no other shield needs the slot. Eat or drop the last of your food as well.

            On the way out, talk to the Odd Old Man in the house east of the Lumber Yard to start Rag and Bone Man I. Chapter 2 collects the bones. His house is in the hills by the limestone mine, west of the Paterdomus temple.

            Done when you hold all 18 materials.

            Coins: about 2,100 gp · Slots: 27/28 · Requirements: none

            Complete Daddy's Home

            Walk to Old Man Yarlo's house in south-east Varrock. Build the five pieces of furniture where the translucent outlines stand, using the hammer and saw:

            • the carpet, with 3 bolts of cloth;
            • two wooden stools, with 2 planks and 4 nails in total;
            • two wooden tables, with 6 planks and 8 nails in total;
            • the wooden chair, with 2 planks and 2 nails;
            • the waxwood bed, with the 3 waxwood planks and 2 bolts of cloth.

            Building them gives 544 Construction XP. Talk to Yarlo, then walk to Marlo in the estate agent's house in north-east Varrock to complete Daddy's Home, for another 400 XP. Since the 3 November 2026 update it counts as a full quest and gives a quest point. 944 Construction XP puts a fresh account at level 8; level 9 is 969 XP and level 10 is 1,154.

            The reward is a player-owned house in Rimmington and Marlo's crate. The crate is a single item that holds 25 planks, 10 oak planks, 8 bolts of cloth, 5 steel bars, 50 mithril nails, 5 house teleport tablets and a Falador teleport tablet. Keep it closed for now; it is one slot until you stand in your house. Drop the leftover steel nails, because the crate's mithril nails replace them. The house matters beyond Tower of Life: Wintertodt only gives Construction XP for brazier repairs to players who own a house, which is the other reason Daddy's Home comes before the Wintertodt session.

            Slots: about 9/28 · Requirements: hammer and saw

            Done when Daddy's Home is complete.

            Finish Gertrude's Cat and push Romeo & Juliet

            With the furniture built and the materials gone, run west to Gertrude's house to complete Gertrude's Cat. She gives you a kitten, a chocolate cake and a stew. Drop the kitten so it follows you instead of taking a slot, and feed it a fish or some milk whenever it says it's hungry, or it eventually runs away. Eat or drop the cake and stew; both can be made again when A Tail of Two Cats and The Queen of Thieves ask for them.

            A kitten needs attention as well as food. It warns you when it gets hungry, roughly 24 minutes after its last meal, and runs off if you ignore it for about six minutes more; it also complains when it is lonely, and stroking it settles it again. A kitten that runs away is gone for good, so if you lose it, Gertrude sells a new one for 100 gp. Chapter 2's cat quests need it, and after about three hours of following you it grows into a cat that needs neither food nor attention. Until then it can't be put in a menagerie, so it stays with you.

            Next door in west Varrock, talk to Juliet and take her letter to Romeo in the square. She is on the first-floor balcony of the mansion just outside the west wall of the city, west of the west bank. If Romeo isn't in the square when you come back, look in the general store just south of it. Then talk to Father Lawrence in the church in north Varrock. The church is north-east of the square. Romeo & Juliet stops there until you have cadava berries in 1.4. The berries grow on bushes just west of the south-east Varrock mine.

            Done when Gertrude's Cat is complete.

            Coins: about 2,100 gp · Slots: about 9/28 · Requirements: none

            Build to 10 Construction in your house

            Open Marlo's crate. Its materials come out noted, so they cost one slot per type, and the house teleports and Falador teleport stack. Break one house teleport to arrive in your new house in Rimmington.

            Tower of Life needs 10 Construction, which is 1,154 XP, and it can't be boosted. You are at 944. Leave the house through the portal and walk to Phials in Rimmington, next to the house portal, and unnote six planks and six bolts of cloth; that fills your inventory to the last slot. Go back in and build torn curtains in your parlour's curtain space (3 planks, 3 bolts of cloth and 3 of the crate's mithril nails), then remove them and build them again. Each build gives 132 XP, which takes you to about 1,208 XP and level 10. Phials stands by the Rimmington general store and charges 5 gp for each item he unnotes, so the twelve items cost you 60 gp.

            You can spend the remaining 19 noted planks on extra furniture if you want a head start toward the 30 Construction this section aims for, but Wintertodt's brazier repairs and Tower of Life cover that anyway. Keep the 5 noted steel bars (clockworks for bird houses in 1.4), the 10 noted oak planks, the remaining house teleports and the Falador teleport. Drop any leftover planks and cloth before Wintertodt if you need the slots.

            Done when you are 10 Construction.

            Coins: about 2,100 gp · Slots: about 15/28 · Requirements: 10 Construction for Tower of Life, unboostable

            Wiki references Children of the Sun Forestry Gertrude's Cat Daddy's Home Rag and Bone Man I Romeo & Juliet Phials

            Tower of Life, Kourend and Wintertodt

            The last block gives you four more Construction levels from Tower of Life, takes Client of Kourend to its last stage, puts on your fourth warm item, and ends with the long Wintertodt session that closes the section. Tower of Life is the last inventory squeeze: its machines are built from parts you collect one machine at a time, about nine to eleven slots each. Before you take the Fishing Trawler teleport, make sure you have that much room.

            Gate in 10 Construction (not boostable) for Tower of Life; 50 Firemaking and four warm items for Wintertodt.
            Carry in the Tower of Life items, then about 12/28 at Wintertodt.
            Exit Tower of Life done; Client of Kourend at its last stage; the clue hunter garb; Wintertodt trained and the Reward Cart's first batch claimed; about 102,000 gp.
            Carries forward the unclaimed Reward Cart batches (logs and herbs later in the chapter).

            Before entering Wintertodt you trim the inventory to what Wintertodt and the start of 1.2 actually need: coins, research notes, spade, hammer, saw, the arrow shafts, the house and Falador teleports, the 5 steel bars and the 10 oak planks, with the Chronicle worn. That is about 10 slots, leaving 18 for roots and kindling plus the knife and tinderbox the crates give you.

            Complete Tower of Life

            Use the Fishing Trawler minigame teleport to reach Port Khazard and walk north to the Tower of Life. Talk to the builders outside to start Tower of Life. The quest needs 10 Construction, which you just reached, and it can't be boosted.

            Inside, you wear the builder's costume for the quest, so move your clue hunter boots into your inventory when the costume's boots go on. The tower has three broken machines; the parts come from the crates in the tower. The pressure machine is repaired by turning its wheels and pulling its levers in a fixed sequence and the cage by fitting bars of the right sizes side by side, so keep the wiki's steps open for both. Get the costume before you go in: the hard hat (answer Black-eye's quiz: 3, torn curtains, 10 clay pieces), the shirt (give the Blue Moon Inn beer to 'The Guns'), the trousers (search the plants south-east of the tower, else south-west) and the boots (pickpocket 'No fingers'); wear all four and give Bonafido the answers 2, 3, 1, 2. The three machines need: the pressure machine (west stairs, north side) 4 valve wheels, 3 metal sheets and 4 coloured balls; the pipe machine (two floors up, south side) 4 pipes, 5 pipe rings and 6 rivets; the cage (top floor) 5 metal bars and 4 binding fluids. Fix one machine at a time and keep 11 free slots with the costume worn (15 without). Never drop a part to make room.

            If you lost or drank the beer, the Flying Horse Inn in East Ardougne sells another for 2 gp. Bonafido's answers, if the order of his options differs from the numbers above, are tea, whistling for attention, cold legs and "carry on, it'll fix itself". The part crates are all on the ground floor, and each crate gives the same part every time, so search the same crate again for the next one. The pressure machine is on the first floor and the pipe machine on the second; the pipe puzzle keeps your progress if you walk away. The costume is only needed to enter while the quest is running. The homunculus at the top of the tower is not a fight: you answer its questions by pushing a bar fully to one side, and either side gives the same result. It then sends you to the basement, where talking to it ends the quest.

            The reward is 2 quest points, 1,000 Construction, 500 Crafting and 500 Thieving XP, which takes Construction from about 1,208 to about 2,208 XP, level 14, before a single Wintertodt brazier. Brazier repairs do the rest of the climb to 30. Put the clue hunter boots back on and drop the builder's costume.

            Done when Tower of Life is complete.

            Coins: about 2,100 gp · Slots: about 14/28 after the quest · Requirements: 10 Construction, unboostable

            Sail to Kourend and start Client of Kourend

            Break a house teleport and walk east from Rimmington to the Port Sarim docks, about a minute. Buy one feather at Gerrant's Fishy Business on the way (neither shop near Veos in Piscarilius sells one). Talk to Veos and ask him to take you to Port Piscarilius in Great Kourend. In Piscarilius, talk to Veos again to start Client of Kourend. He gives you an enchanted scroll; use the feather on it to make the enchanted quill, which writes a reference for each of Kourend's five houses.

            Veos waits on the same northern dock by the Rusty Anchor where you gave him the casket. A stripy or coloured feather also makes the quill, but a magic gold feather doesn't.

            Slots: about 18/28 with the scroll · Requirements: none

            Done when Client of Kourend is started.

            Visit the five houses

            Talk to the general store owner in each of the five Kourend houses about Veos and write what they tell you on the scroll:

            1. Leenz, in the Port Piscarilius general store, a few steps from Veos.
            2. Horace, in the Hosidius general store.
            3. Jennifer, in the Shayzien general store.
            4. Munty, in the Lovakengj general store.
            5. Regath, in the Arceuus general store.

            Use the Lovakengj minecart network to move between the houses instead of running across the whole continent. While you're in Shayzien, do one lap of the Shayzien agility course for the Kourend & Kebos diary. Port Piscarilius has its own station, so you can start riding from there. Each minecart ride costs 20 gp until you complete The Forsaken Tower, so a few rides here barely touch your coins. The agility lap is an easy diary task with no level requirement, and either tier of the course counts. When you talk to Regath, the quill glows and stings your hand; it does no damage, so carry on.

            Return to Veos with the completed scroll. He gives you an orb to activate at the Dark Altar north of Arceuus; take the minecart to the closest station, walk to the altar and use the orb. Stop there. The final conversation with Veos, and the two XP lamps that come with it, happen on your way out of Kourend at the start of 1.2, so the lamps never sit in your inventory through Wintertodt. The altar is at the far end of the area of purple crystals, near fairy ring CIS. The orb shatters when you use it, so you carry nothing extra away. When you finish the quest in 1.2, talk to Veos in Port Piscarilius, not in Port Sarim.

            Slots: about 18/28 with the orb · Requirements: none

            Done when you hold the orb.

            Dig up the clue hunter garb

            Take the minecart to the Shayzien East station. Just north of the station there is a spot with two rocks; stand on it and dig with the spade for the clue hunter garb. Wear it. With the clue hunter gloves and boots and the rainbow scarf you now wear the four warm items Wintertodt asks for, and none of them costs an inventory slot. The garb goes in your body slot. If you ever destroy or lose it, dig on the same spot to get it again. Four is also the most that count: four warm items give the largest cut in the cold damage you take, and each one speeds up how fast your warmth recovers. Enter with fewer and the game warns you at the door.

            Take the minecart to the Northern Tundras station and walk west and then north to the Wintertodt camp, about 30 tiles away. On the way, drop anything that isn't on the trim list at the top of this block.

            Slots: about 11/28 · Requirements: none

            Done when you wear the clue hunter garb.

            Train at Wintertodt

            Walk through the Doors of Dinh. The crates beside the entrance hand out a knife, a tinderbox, a hammer and a bronze axe, but only to players who aren't already carrying one; that is why you dropped your knife and tinderbox after the teaks. Take a knife and a tinderbox. Take one unfinished rejuvenation potion from the crates too, pick a bruma herb from the sprouting roots on the east or west side of the prison, and combine them into a rejuvenation potion. If you haven't done Druidic Ritual, Brew'ma at the entrance combines them for you. At this point you haven't, because Druidic Ritual comes in 1.2, so Brew'ma is your route for now.

            Play a calm single-world game. Chop bruma roots with your wielded steel axe, fletch them into bruma kindling with the knife (kindling gives more points and Fletching XP), and feed the brazier. Light it with the tinderbox when it goes out. Repair it with your hammer when the Wintertodt shatters it; every repair is Construction XP, the cheapest Construction a UIM will ever get. When the pyromancer at your brazier falls, heal him with one dose of your potion. Two hits from the Wintertodt are enough to put him down, and you heal him through the Help option on his right-click menu, with the potion in your inventory. For scale, a root added to the brazier is worth 10 points and a kindling 25, as are each repair and each relight, and a healed pyromancer is worth 75.

            Score at least 500 points every game; below that you get no reward and no bonus XP. That bonus is the bulk of your Firemaking: a game you finish with 500 or more points pays 100 times your Firemaking level in XP, against 6 times your level for each brazier you light. 500 points gives two rewards, 1,000 gives three and 1,500 gives four, and every point past a milestone adds a chance of one more. Restore warmth whenever the meter drops, either with a potion dose (30% warmth) or any food that heals 4 or more (35%). You need no food at all if you keep a potion going. Don't log out or leave the prison before a game ends: you lose every point you scored in that game.

            The cold hits harder as the fight goes on, so watch your warmth meter, not just your Hitpoints: at 0% warmth you die instantly. A death inside the prison leaves your whole inventory on the floor there (and a rainbow scarf is always destroyed on death), and you respawn in Lumbridge, a long trip back against a 60-minute logged-in clock. The two attacks to step away from are the snowfall, which hits a 3x3 area around a player, and the heavy attack that shatters a brazier and damages anyone standing next to it.

            Stay until all three of these are true:

            • 60 Woodcutting. Magic logs can roll in the Reward Cart from 50 Woodcutting and higher levels shift the cart's log table toward better logs; you need 16 magic logs for Desert Treasure and later quests, and the Woodcutting Guild, where 1.3 trains Construction, needs 60.
            • 30 Construction, from brazier repairs on top of Daddy's Home and Tower of Life. Construction is the hardest early skill for a UIM to train anywhere else.
            • 85 Firemaking. Nothing before Chapter 3 needs more than 66, but Chapter 3 needs 75 for Desert Treasure II and the Lumbridge elite diary and 85 for the Kandarin elite diary. The same games also carry the 60 Woodcutting and 30 Construction above and the roughly 450 Reward Cart rolls the batches below are sized on: stopping at 75 would give only about 40% of those games, and stopping at 66 about 16%. Stay longer, up to 99, if you enjoy it; every extra game is also more Reward Cart rolls.

            Fletching from kindling usually lands short of 46 on a single world. If it does, 1.2 finishes it with steel arrows. For scale, the 1,500 shafts from Lumbridge make 1,500 steel arrows, only about 9,000 XP; 46 Fletching is 67,983 XP, so from the low 30s you need several thousand more arrows. Fletch as much kindling as you can here, and plan the Catherby arrowtips as the main cost of 46.

            If a pyromancer piece, warm gloves or a bruma torch drops, wear it in place of a clue hunter piece. A bruma torch is a warm item and a tinderbox in one: in its normal form it takes the weapon slot, so switch it to its off-hand form, wear it in place of the Chronicle and drop the tinderbox; the Chronicle goes into that slot instead. Drop duplicates.

            Slots: about 12/28 while playing · Requirements: 50 Firemaking, four warm items

            Done when your Wintertodt session is done.

            Claim the Reward Cart in batches

            Don't search the Reward Cart while you're still training. It holds up to 8,000 unclaimed rewards, and each reward's loot is rolled at the moment you search, using your levels at that moment. For a UIM, that turns the cart into a storage unit whose contents improve as you level. The cart stands just outside the bank in the Wintertodt camp.

            Coins are about one roll in five, at 2,000 to 4,999 gp each, so they average roughly 700 gp per roll. Going from 50 to 85 Firemaking earns about 450 rolls if you fletch kindling (about 230 games of 500 points; nearer 300 rolls on roots alone). Every roll count in this guide is an estimate, because drops vary per account, so each batch below is sized by what you need to hold when you stop, not by a fixed number of searches. Section 1.2 needs about 100,000 gp before blackjacking takes over as your income (Betty's runes and the feathers are the big costs), which is roughly 145 rolls. Everything else you search in that first batch is dropped on the spot, except two iron ore for Doric's Quest: carrying ten noted loot types through 1.2 would cost you the free slots that Doric's Quest, the Sleeping Giants crate and the banana crate all need. The remaining rolls (about 305 of the estimated 450) stay in the cart and are claimed in 1.4, rolled with higher levels than you have now, exactly when their loot is used. If a later batch runs the cart dry before its keep list is complete, play more Wintertodt, because every extra game adds rolls:

            BatchWhenKeepUsed in
            1When you leave; search until you hold about 100,000 gp (about 145 rolls)Coins and 2 noted iron ore; drop everything elseSection 1.2: runes, feathers, house move, arrowtips, Doric's Quest
            2Early 1.4, in the Fossil Island block; search until you hold about 60 grimy harralander and one grimy ranarrGrimy harralander and one grimy ranarr; drop everything elseSection 1.4: Herblore to 27 and Eadgar's Ruse
            31.4, after the Tithe Farm's seed box; search until you hold 22 mahogany logs and 16 magic logsNoted mahogany and magic logs, herb seeds and noted grimy guam; drop everything elseSection 1.4: Desert Treasure and Watchtower; Chapter 2: Varrock diary planks and the lectern

            Search in groups of five with Big-search, and drop every item type that isn't on the current batch's keep list the moment it appears. Logs, gems, ores, herbs, fish and essence come noted, so each type costs one slot however many you keep; seeds come unnoted but stack. The real limit is the number of different types you hold, not their quantity.

            Done when batch 1 is claimed.

            Coins: about 102,000 gp after batch 1 · Slots: about 13/28

            Wiki references Tower of Life Client of Kourend Lovakengj Minecart Network Clue hunter garb Wintertodt Wintertodt strategies Crate (Wintertodt) Reward Cart

            End of section 1.1

            You leave Wintertodt with 85 Firemaking, 60 Woodcutting, 30 Construction, about 102,000 gp and roughly two-thirds of your estimated Wintertodt rolls still unclaimed in the cart. Section 1.2 starts right here at the camp, so you don't need to travel before it begins.

In progress — Rune Mysteries, Pirate's Treasure, A Porcine of Interest, The Knight's Sword, Romeo & Juliet, Priest in Peril, Family Crest, Rag and Bone Man I, Client of Kourend.

            1.2: Some fishing and thieving

            Section 1.2 takes you from the Wintertodt camp to about 56 Fishing from Tempoross, 46 Fletching, about 53 Agility from the early rooftops and barbarian fishing, about 55 Thieving and a coin stack for section 1.3, with seventeen more quests completed on the way. Two things stand out. First, the rogue outfit moves to 1.3: the Rogues' Den maze needs an empty inventory and nothing worn, and the wiki's UIM advice is to collect it once your house has an armour case to put each piece in. Second, blackjacking therefore stops around 55 Thieving here and resumes, with double loot, in 1.3.

            Tempoross becomes your third deferred storage after Wintertodt's Reward Cart and Veos's lamps. Its reward pool holds up to 8,000 permits and rolls each reward at the moment you collect it, using your Fishing level then.

            Leaving Kourend

            You start 1.2 at the Wintertodt camp with about 13 items: coins, research notes, spade, hammer, saw, knife, tinderbox, the arrow shafts, the house and Falador teleports, 5 noted steel bars, 10 noted oak planks and 2 noted iron ore, with the Chronicle worn. This block finishes Client of Kourend, takes Thieving from about 24 to 41 on the Hosidius fruit stalls, and picks up the one Kourend item the next blocks need: raw rat meat for Druidic Ritual.

            Gate in the Dark Altar visit; 25 Thieving for the fruit stalls.
            Carry in about 13 items from Wintertodt: about 13 to 16/28.
            Exit Client of Kourend done; Thieving trained on the fruit stalls; the rat meat collected and the saltpetre diary task done; about 102,000 gp.
            Carries forward the rat meat (Druidic Ritual).

            Nothing here is dangerous. If something goes wrong, your respawn is still Lumbridge, which is a long way from Kourend, so keep your Hitpoints up around the giant rat and don't fight anything else.

            Finish Client of Kourend

            Take the minecart from the Northern Tundras to the Port Piscarilius station (20 gp a ride) and walk to Veos on the Piscarilius docks. The fare stays at 20 gp until you complete The Forsaken Tower, after which every ride is free, so budget a few rides through this block. The orb shattered into broken glass when you activated it, so just talk to him and finish the dialogue to complete Client of Kourend. Veos claims at first that he hasn't seen you since he brought you to Kourend; keep talking, because his client takes over his voice and hands you the reward before letting him go. It rewards 1 quest point, two antique lamps of 500 XP each, the Kourend Castle Teleport spell and Kharedst's memoirs (destroy the book too: it is a teleport to Kourend's cities that 1.4 uses, and Veos gives you a new copy then; carried until then it would cost the slot Doric's Quest needs). Destroying it now loses nothing: the book only teleports once you add the torn pages that Kourend's quests hand out, and you have none yet. Destroy both lamps, exactly as you did with the X Marks the Spot lamp: Veos now holds three lamps for you, and all of them go on Herblore in 1.3. A lamp can't be stored anywhere and costs a slot for as long as you hold it, which is why destroying it and reclaiming it from Veos on the day you use it is the UIM way to keep it.

            Done when Client of Kourend is complete.

            Coins: about 102,000 gp · Slots: about 13/28 · Requirements: the Dark Altar visit above

            Train Thieving on the Hosidius fruit stalls

            Take the minecart to Hosidius and walk to the building just east of the market square (north of two magic trees), where two unguarded fruit stalls stand next to each other. Use these two and not the four stalls in the square itself: level 44 guard dogs watch the square, and they attack anyone they see stealing. The stalls need 25 Thieving, which Wintertodt's camp left you just short of or at, depending on your silk count; if you're below 25, steal a few cakes from the Kourend Castle courtyard bakery first. The bakery stall needs only 5 Thieving and gives 16 XP a cake, so a short session there covers the gap.

            Steal from the two stalls in turn. Each successful steal gives 28.5 Thieving XP and a random fruit. A stall restocks 2.4 seconds after you empty it, which is why you alternate: by the time you have taken from the second, the first is ready again. Your first steal also completes the Kourend & Kebos easy diary's fruit stall task. Drop every fruit as it appears, with two exceptions. Strange fruit restores run energy, so keep two or three and eat them on the long runs later in this section. Each one restores 30% run energy and heals nothing, and each takes its own slot, so three is the most worth carrying. Papayas heal a little; eat them if a stall owner catches you, otherwise drop them. Don't keep golovanova tops or jangerberries: both are re-stolen in seconds from these same stalls when you need them in 1.4.

            Keep going to 41 Thieving (41,171 XP), which is roughly 1,170 steals from 25. 41 covers every Thieving requirement before blackjacking: Ethically Acquired Antiquities needs 25, The Feud needs 30 to start, and Death on the Isle needs more than both. Death on the Isle's 34 is unboostable, so it matters that you pass it here rather than count on a boost later. The Feud's 15,000 Thieving XP and Ethically Acquired Antiquities' 6,000 then take you to about 45, the level where blackjacking bearded bandits starts.

            Done when the fruit-stall session is done.

            Coins: about 102,000 gp · Slots: about 15/28 with strange fruit · Requirements: 25 Thieving

            Collect the rat meat, dig saltpetre and visit the estate agent

            Walk north of Hosidius town square and kill a giant rat for its raw rat meat. That is one of the four raw meats Druidic Ritual needs in Taverley; the other three are bought or killed near Falador, so this is the only one you carry across the sea. The rats there are level 6 and aggressive, so expect one to attack you as you arrive, and every Kourend giant rat drops raw rat meat. Take nothing else from the rat.

            UIM risk The meat is the one item from this block that rides all the way to Taverley, so don't drop it by accident when you trim the kit in Port Sarim; if you lose it, the giant rats north of the Port Sarim chapel drop another.

            Walk to the five saltpetre deposits north-east of the Woodcutting Guild in Hosidius and dig one up with your spade for the Kourend & Kebos easy diary, then drop it. Only one of the five deposits holds saltpetre at a time, and it moves to another once dug. Konoo follows the live deposit, so look for his yellow dot on the minimap rather than trying each deposit in turn. You don't need the Hosidius estate agent: moving a house there takes 25 Construction and 8,750 gp, and you move it much later.

            Return to the Piscarilius docks and ask Veos to take you to Port Sarim.

            Done when you hold the rat meat and the saltpetre task is done.

            Coins: about 102,000 gp · Slots: about 16/28 · Requirements: none

            Wiki references Client of Kourend Fruit Stall Kourend & Kebos Diary Ultimate Ironman Guide/Thieving

            Port Sarim, Rimmington and Falador

            This block is the main shopping stop of the section. It finishes Pirate's Treasure, moves your house to Pollnivneach, and gathers every ingredient the four Taverley quests in the next block need. The ingredients are what make it tight: by the time you leave Falador you hold about 26 items, and the next block hands them in one quest at a time until Doric's Quest, the hungriest of the four, has the twelve slots it needs.

            Gate in a spade; about 75,000 gp for runes, feathers, ingredients and the house move.
            Carry in the trimmed kit, then every Taverley ingredient: about 26/28 by Falador.
            Exit Pirate's Treasure done; the house moved to Pollnivneach; every ingredient for the four Taverley quests held; about 70,500 gp.
            Carries forward the Taverley quest ingredients.

            The trip is a loop: Port Sarim, west to Rimmington, north to Falador. Walk it in that order; going back for a forgotten onion costs more than the whole loop.

            Trim the Wintertodt kit

            Before you buy anything, make room. Drop the saw: nothing before 1.3 builds furniture, and the saw that spawns in the Varrock estate agent's house is free whenever you want it back. Drop the tinderbox; Tempoross and the Trawler provide their own fires, and the general stores sell one for a coin. Drop the 10 noted oak planks too. Don't keep them for 1.3: the Tempoross reward pool and the Varrock sawmill both give oak planks right when 1.3 needs them, and in between they'd cost a slot through the whole section. Eat your strange fruit when your run energy is low and drop the rest.

            Keep the 5 noted steel bars. They are the five clockworks for your first bird houses in 1.4, and you can't smelt steel yet.

            Your core kit for the rest of 1.2 is now 10 items: coins, research notes, spade, hammer, knife, house teleports, Falador teleport, steel bars, the arrow-shaft stack and the raw rat meat, with the Chronicle worn. The noted iron ore (one slot) makes it 11. If you skipped the arrow shafts in Lumbridge, you have one slot more than the counts below. The iron ore is the one noted item in the kit that you unnote on purpose: Doric wants two iron ore, iron needs 15 Mining, and you reach only 10 Mining from Doric's own reward, so bringing these two ore is how you hand them in this early.

            Slots: about 11/28

            Done when your kit is down to about 11 slots.

            Buy runes, feathers and the quest ingredients in Port Sarim

            Veos drops you on the Port Sarim docks. You don't have a rune pouch yet, so every rune type costs a slot, and this guide buys only four types. In Betty's magic shop, in the north-west corner of the town, buy about 500 air, 1,000 water, 1,000 mind and 150 chaos runes as single runes, for about 22,500 gp, most of it the chaos runes. Betty keeps 5,000 of each elemental and mind rune but only 250 chaos runes at 90 gp each, so if another player has just bought her out, hop worlds rather than wait for the restock. That is Water Strike now and Water Bolt at 23 Magic, the spells the experiments, warlords and Feud fighters ahead are weakest to, with enough margin to train Magic as you quest. Water Strike needs 5 Magic, and Tutorial Island plus Witch's Potion leave a fresh account at about 4: before you leave Port Sarim, cast Wind Strike on something harmless (a seagull on the docks) until you reach 5. Earth runes for the Waterfall chalice are bought later, when the quest is in reach.

            In Gerrant's Fishy Business, buy feather packs and open each one before buying the next, so the feathers merge into a single stack. Each pack holds 100 feathers and costs 200 gp, the same 2 gp a feather as buying them loose; the packs only save clicks once Gerrant's stock of 1,000 loose feathers runs low. Buy about 2,000 feathers: roughly 300 for fly fishing to 35 at Seers' Village and 1,500 for headless arrows if you made shafts in Lumbridge. The roughly 21,000 more (14,000 for barbarian fishing and 7,000 for the extra Catherby arrows) are bought on your second pass through Port Sarim, in the Thurgo block, so they don't ride through Taverley as a reason to keep coins you might need.

            In Wydin's Food Store, buy one raw beef and one raw chicken for Druidic Ritual, one cheese for Witch's House and three redberries for Aggie's red dye. Wydin stocks only one raw beef, one raw chicken and one redberry at a time (cheese is three) and restocks about once a minute, so wait or hop worlds for the second and third redberry.

            Done when the Port Sarim purchases are done.

            Coins: about 77,500 gp · Slots: about 22/28 · Requirements: none

            Pick the onions in Rimmington

            Walk west to Rimmington and pick two onions from the field just north of the village. With the three redberries, they let Aggie make the yellow and red dyes that combine into orange for Goblin Diplomacy. The field has 15 onions and each one regrows about 30 seconds after it is picked, so even on a busy world you won't wait long.

            Slots: about 24/28

            Done when you hold the onions.

            Finish Pirate's Treasure and buy the woad leaves

            Walk north to Falador and into the park. Dig at the spot the Blue Moon Inn message described to complete Pirate's Treasure. The spot is near the statue of Saradomin in the middle of the park: look for the patch of dirt that roughly forms an X, stand in its centre and dig. A gardener attacks you the moment you dig; he is level 4, so kill him with a couple of Water Strikes and dig again. You can't dig again while he is alive; if you would rather not fight, walk out of the park until he despawns and then come back to the same spot. The casket holds coins, a gold ring and an emerald. Keep the coins and drop the ring and the emerald: you have no slots to spare, and the Al Kharid gem trader sells the few gems 1.3 needs.

            Find Wyson, the park's head gardener, and offer him 20 gp for woad leaves. He gives you two, exactly what Aggie needs for one blue dye. Choose the 20 gp offer in his dialogue: 15 gp buys only one leaf, and you would have to talk to him again for the second.

            Done when Pirate's Treasure is complete and you hold the woad leaves.

            Coins: about 78,000 gp · Slots: about 25/28 with the woad leaves (they stack in one slot), the casket loot dropped · Requirements: spade

            Move your house to Pollnivneach

            Talk to the Falador estate agent and pay 7,500 gp to move your house to Pollnivneach. From now on every house teleport tablet from Marlo's crate drops you in the desert town where you blackjack, a few steps from the bandits and from the bar that sells their food. It turns the tablets you already carry into free trips to your training spot. The move needs 20 Construction, which your Wintertodt repairs passed long ago, and boosts can't be used for it. The town itself has no desert heat, so arriving by tablet costs you no water, while the magic carpet from the Shantay Pass would cost 200 gp every trip.

            Don't take the Knight's Sword portrait now: you pass back through Falador on the way to Thurgo with the iron bars, and the portrait then goes straight from Sir Vyvin's cupboard to Thurgo instead of riding with you through Taverley, Varrock and Varlamore.

            Done when your house is in Pollnivneach.

            Coins: about 70,500 gp · Slots: about 26/28 · Requirements: none

            Wiki references Betty Gerrant's Fishy Business Wydin's Food Store Pirate's Treasure The Knight's Sword Estate agent

            Draynor dyes and the Taverley quests

            This block completes Goblin Diplomacy, Druidic Ritual, Witch's House and Doric's Quest, unlocks Herblore, and starts Fishing Contest and Below Ice Mountain. It is built as a chain of hand-ins. You arrive with about 26 slots filled, and the order below makes each quest return the space the next one needs: Aggie turns seven ingredients into two dyes, Druidic Ritual eats four raw meats, Witch's House the cheese, Goblin Diplomacy the dyes, and that leaves Doric's Quest exactly the twelve slots it needs for its ores.

            Gate in the kit and every ingredient (about 26 slots); Water Strike (5 Magic); 16 quest points for Below Ice Mountain.
            Carry in the ingredients, used in the order below: about 26/28 falling to about 16/28.
            Exit Goblin Diplomacy, Druidic Ritual, Witch's House and Doric's Quest done; 30 Agility from the Draynor and Al Kharid rooftops; Herblore unlocked; Fishing Contest and Below Ice Mountain started; about 70,600 gp.
            Carries forward Fishing Contest's next steps.

            The fights here are the Witch's experiment, four forms from level 19 to 53, and the suits of armour in Taverley Dungeon. All of them are weak to Water Strike, and the experiment's last two forms can be safespotted.

            Have Aggie make the dyes

            Walk south-east from Falador to Draynor Village and find Aggie in her house, at the end of a short dead-end road that branches off the east road through the village. Use each set of ingredients on her rather than going through her dialogue: the two onions for yellow dye, the three redberries for red dye and the two woad leaves for blue dye, 5 gp each. Mix the red and yellow dyes together into orange. Mix them with each other and not on a goblin mail: red dye used on a yellow mail, or yellow on a red one, gives you a red or yellow mail, not an orange one.

            You now hold an orange dye and a blue dye in place of seven ingredients.

            Done when you hold the dyes.

            Coins: about 70,400 gp · Slots: about 21/28 · Requirements: none

            Rooftops to 30: Draynor and Al Kharid

            Most of 1.2 is on foot, and run energy is what runs out first. Before you go north for the bear, put an hour and a half into the rooftop courses: Draynor to 20 Agility, then Al Kharid to 30. Varrock's course takes you to 40 in the next block, and the marks of grace you collect on all three go towards the graceful outfit in 1.3.

            The Draynor course starts at the rough wall next to the wheat field north of Draynor's bank. Since the May 2024 update it has no Agility requirement and none of its obstacles can fail. A lap is 120 XP and takes about 43 seconds, roughly 10,000 XP an hour, so 1 to 20 Agility (4,470 XP) is about 37 laps, or half an hour. Each lap ends when you climb down a crate off Ned's house, and most of its XP (79 of the 120) comes from that last crate, so finish every lap you start.

            Pick up every mark of grace you see on the rooftops. Marks despawn after 10 minutes and stack in one slot; keep the stack until you buy the graceful outfit after Priest in Peril in 1.3. The full outfit costs 260 marks, so the roughly 30 you bring out of this section are a head start, not the whole bill. Moving on at the levels this guide gives is also what keeps marks coming: once your Agility is 20 or more above a course's requirement, the chance of a mark on each lap drops by 80%.

            At 20, run east through Lumbridge and pay the 10 gp toll at the Al Kharid gate; Prince Ali Rescue, which waives it, isn't done yet. The Al Kharid course starts at the rough wall on the north side of Ellis the Tanner's building. It needs 20 Agility and gives 216 XP a lap of about 64 seconds, up to roughly 12,000 XP an hour, so 20 to 30 (about 8,900 XP) is about 42 laps, or 45 minutes. The first tightrope and the zip line can fail for 1 to 5 damage; if your Hitpoints drop low, stop and let them regenerate instead of buying food you would have to carry. Each lap ends with a jump off a building east of Zeke's Superior Scimitars, so the walk back to the rough wall is short.

            Run back west through Lumbridge and Draynor.

            Done when you are 30 Agility.

            Coins: about 70,390 gp · Slots: about 22/28 with the marks of grace · Requirements: 20 Agility for Al Kharid

            Kill a bear for its meat

            Walk back north through Falador and out of its north gate. North of Falador, where the path meets the trees, there are bears; kill one from behind the trees so it can't reach you. These are the four black bears outside the Mind Altar ruins, east of Goblin Village and short of Ice Mountain. A black bear is level 19, hits no more than 3 and isn't aggressive, so you pick the one you fight. Use Water Strike from a spot it can't path to, then take the raw bear meat. That is the fourth Druidic Ritual meat. Every black bear drops one, so if a second player takes the kill from you, wait for the respawn rather than look elsewhere.

            Slots: about 23/28 · Requirements: Water Strike (5 Magic)

            Done when you hold the bear meat.

            Complete Druidic Ritual

            Walk west into Taverley and north to the stone circle, and talk to Kaqemeex to start Druidic Ritual. He sends you to Sanfew, upstairs in the house south of the circle, who asks for four raw meats dipped in the Cauldron of Thunder. Sanfew's house is the large octagonal Herblore shop in the south of the village, marked with a herb icon on the minimap; tell him you were sent to help.

            The cauldron is in Taverley Dungeon, whose ladder is just south of the village. Head north past the skeletons to the gate on the dungeon's east side, flanked by the two suits of armour. The skeletons on the way are level 22 and 25, so keep running and don't stop to fight them. Two suits of armour come to life when you open it; kill them with Water Strike or run past them. They are level 19 and attack whatever your combat level, so if you would rather not fight, keep clicking the gate until it opens and step through. Use each of the four meats on the cauldron: raw beef, raw rat meat, raw bear meat and raw chicken. Each piece turns into its enchanted, blue version as you use it, so check that all four have changed before you climb out. Climb out, give the four enchanted meats to Sanfew, and talk to Kaqemeex to complete the quest. It unlocks Herblore and gives your first 250 Herblore XP.

            Slots: about 19/28 · Requirements: none

            Done when Druidic Ritual is complete.

            Complete Witch's House

            Talk to the boy by the hedges near Taverley's eastern entrance to start Witch's House; his ball is locked in the witch's shed. Find the key hidden by the house's front door and let yourself in. Walk west and then north around the hedge to reach the door, and search the pot just north of it for the key.

            Go down to the basement. With your gloves on, open the gate; any gloves work, including your clue hunter gloves, and the boxes in the basement give leather gloves if you have none. The ladder down is in the northern room. Search the cupboard behind the gate for a magnet. Go back upstairs to the small room on the south side, drop your cheese there and use the magnet on the mouse the moment it appears; it opens the door to the garden. Be quick: if the mouse goes back into its hole before you use the magnet, the cheese is gone with it. You bought only one, so if that happens, the nearest replacements are the cheese that spawns in Aggie's house in Draynor and Wydin's in Port Sarim.

            In the garden, keep out of the witch's sight, because if she sees you she teleports you out and you start the garden again. She walks back and forth through the middle, so go round the edges behind the hedges. Get the shed key and open the shed. The key is in the fountain: search it. Don't touch the ball yet: attack the Witch's experiment first. Taking the ball early lowers your stats. It has four forms, level 19, 30, 42 and 53, and you must kill all four without leaving. If you step out of the shed mid-fight, the experiment starts again from its first form, and if the witch sees you in the shed she teleports you out, takes the ball back and you have to unlock the shed again. For the third and fourth forms, stand between the sacks and the crate with the ball on it, right against the wall, and cast Water Strike from there; they can't reach you. That spot is on the shed's south wall. Then take the ball and give it back to the boy to complete the quest, for 6,325 Hitpoints XP.

            Slots: about 18/28 after the temporary keys and magnet are gone · Requirements: gloves, cheese

            Done when Witch's House is complete.

            Complete Goblin Diplomacy

            Walk to Goblin Village, north of Falador and east of Taverley, and talk to the two goblin generals to start Goblin Diplomacy. They argue about the colour of their armour and want three goblin mails to choose from. Collect three goblin mails in the village; goblins there drop them, and three named crates in the village each hold one. One crate is behind the generals' hut, one inside the western hut between the small square hut and the generals' hut, and one up a ladder near the village gate.

            Use the orange dye on one goblin mail and the blue dye on another. Leave the third one plain: if you dye it by mistake, its crate won't give you another, and you'd have to kill goblins until one drops a mail. Give the generals the orange mail first, then the blue one, then the plain one. They settle on the plain brown, and the quest completes, giving you 200 Crafting XP and a gold bar. Drop the gold bar: Doric's Quest needs that slot in a few minutes, and the Waterfall Quest and The Dig Site give you the gold bars your 1.3 jewellery needs.

            Slots: about 16/28 · Requirements: none

            Done when Goblin Diplomacy is complete.

            Complete Doric's Quest

            On your way to Doric, stop at the Falador west bank and unnote your noted iron ore at a bank booth into two ore; that is the one thing a UIM may still do at a bank. Walk north to Doric's hut and talk to him to start Doric's Quest. His hut is the small house at the crossroads north of Falador, east of the Taverley gate and south of Goblin Village. He wants 6 clay, 4 copper ore and 2 iron ore, all at once and unnoted, and gives you a bronze pickaxe to mine them. He asks for plain clay, not soft clay, so don't wet it.

            Walk east to the Dwarven Mine and climb down. From Doric's hut, go east and then north up the path onto Ice Mountain; the ladder down is among the buildings and dwarves on the mountain. The copper rocks are just south of the ladder, and the clay rocks a little further south; both can be mined at 1 Mining. Mine four copper and six clay. With the pickaxe that is 28 slots exactly. Walk back to Doric and hand everything over to complete the quest, for 1,300 Mining XP, enough to take a fresh account straight to 10 Mining, and 180 gp. He takes the whole set in one hand-in, so with 28 slots full, leave anything else on the ground until he has it.

            Keep the bronze pickaxe. The Knight's Sword needs 10 Mining for blurite, which you now have, and a pickaxe to mine it.

            Done when Doric's Quest is complete.

            Coins: about 70,600 gp · Slots: about 16/28 · Requirements: none; 10 Mining afterwards

            Start Fishing Contest and Below Ice Mountain

            Walk north from Taverley to Austri at the eastern entrance of the White Wolf Mountain tunnel and talk to him to start Fishing Contest. He gives you a fishing pass, which you take to Hemenster in the Catherby block. The quest needs 10 Fishing to start and it can't be boosted; Sea Slug's 7,175 Fishing XP already took you well past it. The pass is the only item this start adds, and it rides with you until the contest itself.

            Finally, walk back south-east toward Ice Mountain and talk to Willow to start Below Ice Mountain. She stands on the path south of Ice Mountain, north-east of Falador and just south of the Dwarven Mine entrance you used for Doric. It needs 16 quest points to start, which you passed during this block. It continues much later; this is just the start. The start is a conversation and gives you nothing to carry; the three recruits and the Camdozaal fight are in Chapter 2, together with Black Knights' Fortress. Teleport to Varrock with the Chronicle.

            Slots: about 17/28 · Requirements: 16 quest points for Below Ice Mountain

            Done when both quests are started.
            Wiki references Aggie Druidic Ritual Witch's House Goblin Diplomacy Doric's Quest Fishing Contest Below Ice Mountain

            Varrock and Varlamore

            This block puts a staff of air in your weapon slot, completes Shield of Arrav and starts Ethically Acquired Antiquities, which needs Shield of Arrav finished first. You arrive with about 17 items and leave with about 20, the two iron bars included: the staff swaps your air runes for the steel axe in the inventory, and the only new carry is a pie dish for Thurgo's redberry pie.

            Gate in a partner from the other gang for Shield of Arrav; Children of the Sun and 25 Thieving for Ethically Acquired Antiquities.
            Carry in about 17/28.
            Exit a staff of air wielded; 40 Agility from the Varrock rooftops; Shield of Arrav done; Ethically Acquired Antiquities started; about 69,700 gp.
            Carries forward the pie dish (the redberry pie).

            Shield of Arrav is the one quest in this route that needs another player. Do it in a single sitting once you've found a partner, so the half shield never sits in your inventory waiting.

            Wield a staff of air

            The Chronicle drops you at the Champions' Guild. Walk into Varrock to Zaff's Superior Staffs and buy a staff of air. Wield it: it supplies unlimited air runes for every Strike and Bolt spell, so you can drop your air rune stack straight away. The steel axe moves into your inventory, so the swap costs nothing. The staff costs 1,500 gp and Zaff keeps only two in stock, so if both are gone, hop worlds rather than wait for the restock.

            Done when you wield a staff of air.

            Coins: about 69,100 gp · Slots: about 17/28 · Requirements: none

            Varrock rooftops to 40

            Before the palace, train on the Varrock Rooftop Course to 40 Agility. Climb the rough wall on the east side of the Varrock General Store to start. It needs 30 Agility and gives about 270 XP a lap of about 1 minute 10 seconds, roughly 14,000 XP and 8 to 11 marks of grace an hour. The clothes line (3 to 8 damage) and one wall (2 to 5) can fail; as in Al Kharid, wait out low Hitpoints rather than carry food. 30 to 40 is about 23,900 XP, roughly 88 laps or just under two hours, and you should finish with around 30 marks. Each lap ends with a jump off the house south of Lowe's Archery Emporium, and your first full lap completes a Varrock medium diary task.

            40 Agility is also what the Canifis course needs, and Canifis is where you finish the graceful outfit in 1.3.

            Done when you are 40 Agility.

            Coins: about 69,100 gp · Slots: about 17/28 with the marks · Requirements: 30 Agility

            Pick up the pie dish

            In Varrock Palace, take the pie dish from the kitchen. It is for the redberry pie that buys Thurgo's help with The Knight's Sword in the next block. While you're in the palace, talk to Reldo and ask about quests to start Shield of Arrav; read the book he points you to and talk to him again. Reldo is in the northernmost room of the palace library, and the book is a blue-and-white one on the top shelf of the bookcase east of the short bookcases in the middle of the room.

            Slots: about 18/28

            Done when you hold the pie dish.

            Complete Shield of Arrav with a partner

            Shield of Arrav needs two players, one in each of two gangs, who swap a key and some crossbows. Join the clan chat "osrs soa" and ask for a partner from the other gang. Join the Black Arm Gang, because Phoenix players looking for a Black Arm partner are more common, so you find one faster.

            • Black Arm Gang. Talk to Charlie the Tramp next to Varrock's southern entrance, at the end of the alley to the Black Arm hideout, then go in and talk to Katrine. She wants two Phoenix crossbows from the Phoenix weapon store. Get the weapon store key from your Phoenix partner, kill the Weaponsmaster inside with Water Strike, and take two Phoenix crossbows to Katrine, who makes you a gang member. The weapon store is reached by a ladder in a small room directly south of the rune shop, the room attached to the chapel; the Weaponsmaster is level 23 and attacks as soon as you try to take the crossbows. Then go upstairs in the Black Arm hideout and take your half of the shield from the cupboard there.
            • Phoenix Gang. Talk to Baraek in Varrock square and pay him 20 gp to learn about the gang. In the Phoenix hideout, talk to Straven and accept his task. Go into the Blue Moon Inn and kill Jonny the Beard with Water Strike (don't wear a ring of recoil, or his report won't drop), take the report back to Straven, and receive the weapon store key. Jonny can only be attacked once Straven has given you the task, so talk to Straven first. The hideout's entrance is down the alley past the Blue Moon Inn, by the ladder in the room on its south side. Your half of the shield is in the chest in the south-west corner of the Phoenix hideout.

            Meet your partner at the Varrock Museum. Each of you gives your own half of the shield to Curator Haig Halen, who gives you each a half-certificate; swap one half-certificate with your partner, then give the full certificate to King Roald to complete Shield of Arrav for 600 gp. As an ironman you can't trade, so use your half-certificate on your partner instead; the game swaps them without the trade window. Combine the two halves into the full certificate before you walk to the palace. Every quest item from it leaves your inventory at that hand-in. The reward also includes an antique lamp (500 XP in any skill): destroy it like the others, and King Roald gives it back whenever you want it.

            Done when Shield of Arrav is complete.

            Coins: about 69,700 gp · Slots: about 18/28 · Requirements: a partner from the other gang

            Start Ethically Acquired Antiquities in Varlamore

            Ethically Acquired Antiquities needs Children of the Sun, Shield of Arrav and 25 Thieving, all of which you now have. Run out of Varrock's east gate to Regulus Cento and take the quetzal to Civitas illa Fortis. The quest needs no items and has no fights, and its quest items come and go within a few steps of each other.

            In the Grand Museum in the north of the city, inspect the empty display case in the north-east corner to start Ethically Acquired Antiquities, then talk to Curator Herminius in the middle of the museum about it. Investigate the tools behind the case, inspect the case again, ask the visitors in the museum, talk to Curator Herminius once more, and then ask Regulus Cento at the landing site. The visitor who saw the thief is chosen at random for each player, so keep asking the citizens, academics and tourists until one says a shifty man left by the front door towards the landing site. The Regulus Cento you need here is the one at the Civitas landing site, not the one outside Varrock. Walk to the Fortis Cothon and talk to a trader crewmember about the man who carried a case; have Artima repair their sail, then talk to the crewmember again. They tell you he sailed to Port Sarim. Keep one slot free before you talk to the crew: the tattered sail they hand you is a quest item, and Artima's shop is just south of the Cothon.

            The quest's next stops are Port Sarim and the Varrock Museum, both on your path in the next block. Before you leave Varlamore, collect the two iron bars the next block needs from the single safe spawns upstairs in the Shields of Mistrock and on the Sunset Coast (each respawns after a few minutes, so wait or hop for the second if you take both from one spawn). Take the quetzal back to Varrock.

            Slots: about 20/28 · Requirements: Children of the Sun, Shield of Arrav, 25 Thieving

            Done when Ethically Acquired Antiquities is started.
            Wiki references Zaff's Superior Staffs Shield of Arrav Ethically Acquired Antiquities Grand Museum

            Iron bars, Thurgo and The Knight's Sword

            This block completes The Knight's Sword and Ethically Acquired Antiquities. The Knight's Sword alone takes Smithing from 1 to 29, which is what lets you start Sleeping Giants in the desert next. You arrive in Varrock with about 20 items including the two iron bars from Varlamore, pick up the pie ingredients, the portrait and the blurite ore in that order, and hand each in within the same trip. Nothing new leaves the block with you.

            Gate in two iron bars; 10 Cooking for the pie; 10 Mining for blurite.
            Carry in about 20 to 22/28.
            Exit The Knight's Sword and Ethically Acquired Antiquities done; Smithing about 29; about 25,000 gp.
            Carries forward nothing new.

            The one real danger is the Asgarnia Ice Dungeon. Its ice warriors (level 57) and ice giants (level 53) are aggressive and hit hard for your level, so you run past them rather than fight.

            UIM risk Your respawn is Lumbridge and a death there leaves your pile in the dungeon, so go in with full Hitpoints.

            Check your two iron bars

            The Knight's Sword needs two iron bars, and you can't smelt iron until 15 Smithing. You collected two iron bars in Varlamore at the end of the previous block, from the spawns upstairs in the Shields of Mistrock and on the Sunset Coast. The only Wilderness iron bar spawn is north-west of Ferox Enclave, so don't fetch one there: a UIM death in the Wilderness hands everything to the killer.

            Slots: about 20/28 · Requirements: none

            Done when you hold two iron bars.

            Bake the redberry pie

            Break the Falador teleport tablet from Marlo's crate and walk south to Port Sarim. Before anything else, pick up the Port Sarim leads for Ethically Acquired Antiquities, since you're here anyway: on the southernmost dock, talk to Trader Stan or one of his crewmembers about the grey-haired man with the case. They tell you he headed north looking for runes. In Betty's magic shop, talk to Betty, read the note she gives you, and destroy it once read. Have a free slot when you talk to her, because she hands you the note during the conversation; the note is what tells you the man wanted the runes for a Varrock Teleport, which is why the trail now leads to the Varrock Museum. While you're at her counter, buy 6 air runes and 6 earth runes: with the water runes you already carry, they are the 6 of each that the Waterfall chalice takes later in this section, and the staff of air doesn't count for that. Then walk into Gerrant's and buy the remaining feathers, about 21,000: 14,000 for barbarian fishing and 7,000 for the Catherby arrows, opening each pack as you buy it so they join your existing stack. Expect the feathers to take roughly 45,000 to 55,000 gp. Buy one fly fishing rod here too, for 5 gp: Harry's Fishing Shop in Catherby sells neither fly fishing rods nor feathers. It costs one slot until then.

            In Wydin's Food Store, buy two pots of flour and two redberries; the second set is insurance, since you can burn the pie at your Cooking level. At level 10 a range cooks the pie successfully only about half the time, and you are only a few levels above that, so the spare set may well be used. In Rimmington, buy a jug at the general store and fill it at the well.

            Use the jug of water on a pot of flour to make pastry dough, use the dough on your pie dish to make a pie shell, and add a redberry. Cook the uncooked pie on the range in the house next to the general store, the same range you burned the beef on in 1.1. A redberry pie needs 10 Cooking: Gertrude's Cat took you to level 11, and Pirate's Treasure, which now also pays 800 Cooking XP, lifts you to about level 14. If it burns, empty the dish and make the second one. Drop the jug and any spare flour or redberries once you have a cooked pie. Thurgo takes only a whole pie, so don't eat half of it if your Hitpoints drop on the walk to his hut.

            Done when you hold the redberry pie.

            Coins: about 20,000 gp · Slots: about 22/28 with the pie and the two new rune stacks · Requirements: 10 Cooking

            Bribe Thurgo and take the portrait

            Walk south-west from Port Sarim to Mudskipper Point and find Thurgo in his hut. His hut is on the west side of the hill south of Port Sarim, with an anvil beside it. Give him the redberry pie, then ask him about the sword. He can make one, but needs a picture of it to copy. That conversation is what makes the squire tell you about the portrait, so walk north to Falador now and talk to the squire in the White Knights' Castle courtyard.

            Climb the ladder on the east side of the castle to the first floor, walk west and climb the staircase. From the courtyard that means going east through three doors to the ladder, then west through a door and an open doorway to the staircase. Sir Vyvin's bedroom is directly south. Wait until he walks out or turns away, then open and search the cupboard for the portrait. If he catches you, he stops you and you try again once he's looking elsewhere. If he stays in the room facing the cupboard, hop worlds until you find one where he is outside it.

            Walk back to Thurgo and give him the portrait. He now needs one blurite ore and the two iron bars.

            Slots: about 22/28 with the portrait, the pie gone · Requirements: none

            Done when you hold the portrait.

            Mine the blurite and complete The Knight's Sword

            The trapdoor into the Asgarnia Ice Dungeon is on the hill just east of Thurgo's hut. Climb down with full Hitpoints. Follow the dungeon until you reach the icy area with the ice warriors and ice giants, and keep moving along the southern wall to the blurite rocks. Every monster in this dungeon is aggressive, including the level 26 pirates you meet first: from the ladder, run north until you reach them, take the first corridor east and enter the large icy room. The blurite rocks are at its south-eastern end, beside the entrance to the skeletal wyvern area, and standing directly in front of that entrance is a safespot from which you can mine without the giants and warriors reaching you. Mine one blurite ore with the bronze pickaxe from Doric; it needs 10 Mining, which Doric's Quest gave you. Don't stop to fight anything; run straight back to the trapdoor. If you do die here, your pile stays in the dungeon among aggressive monsters, so run back from Lumbridge at full Hitpoints and pick up the pile before doing anything else.

            Give Thurgo the blurite ore and the two iron bars and he makes the blurite sword. Walk north to Falador one last time and give the sword to the squire to complete The Knight's Sword, for 12,725 Smithing XP. On a fresh account that takes Smithing from 1 to 29. Keep the sword in your inventory: the squire won't take it while you are wielding it.

            Drop the bronze pickaxe now. Its only remaining job would be Tourist Trap's mining camp in the next block, and there it matters only if a guard catches you out of your slave robes and sends you to mine 15 rocks; the wiki's alternative to the rocks is to teleport out, and the slot is worth more at the camp gate. The next pickaxe you need is a better one, bought in 1.3.

            Slots: about 18/28 with the pickaxe dropped · Requirements: 10 Mining

            Done when The Knight's Sword is complete.

            Recover the diadem and finish Ethically Acquired Antiquities

            From Falador, teleport to Varrock with the Chronicle.

            Walk to the Varrock Museum. Pickpocket Curator Haig Halen for the storeroom key. Following the wiki's order, tell him first that you are looking for Xerna's Diadem; he acts suspicious and refuses to help, and then you take the key from him. Open the storeroom on the ground floor and search the single unstacked crate inside for Xerna's Diadem. The storeroom is in the north-east of the museum, directly under the stairs to the first floor. Confront Haig with it; the dialogue has a shame meter, and you keep pressing him until he agrees to return the diadem. Options that call him a thief or criticise his museum usually raise the meter, and some of the others lower it; once it is full, offer to leave his part out of your report in return for the diadem going back.

            Run to Regulus Cento, take the quetzal to Civitas illa Fortis, and talk to Curator Herminius in the Grand Museum to complete Ethically Acquired Antiquities, for 6,000 Thieving XP and 5,000 gp. Take the quetzal straight back to Varrock.

            Done when Ethically Acquired Antiquities is complete.

            Coins: about 25,000 gp · Slots: about 18/28 · Requirements: none

            Wiki references Ferox Enclave Redberry pie The Knight's Sword Asgarnia Ice Dungeon Ethically Acquired Antiquities Curator Haig Halen

            Al Kharid and the desert

            This block completes The Feud and Tourist Trap, starts Prince Ali Rescue, and gets your first blackjacking session in Pollnivneach, to 55 Thieving. The Feud's 15,000 Thieving XP is the biggest single jump of the section, and its oak blackjack is what unlocks blackjacking at all.

            Gate in 10 Fletching and 20 Smithing for Tourist Trap; 30 Thieving for The Feud; 45 Thieving and a blackjack for bandits.
            Carry in about 18 to 22/28, 26 for a moment at the mining camp gate; wines while blackjacking.
            Exit Prince Ali Rescue started; Tourist Trap and The Feud done; 55 Thieving; about 125,000 gp.
            Carries forward the willow blackjack.

            One quest from the desert trip moves out of this section: Sleeping Giants. For a UIM, searching the crate in the Giants' Foundry needs 20 empty inventory slots at once (for a short time), which is awkward with the kit you carry here. In 1.3 and 1.4 there are moments with a light inventory, and Sleeping Giants waits for one of those in 1.4: a planned death in the Al Kharid mine, next to the foundry. The Observatory moves to 1.4 for a similar reason: it needs three planks, a bronze bar and a molten glass, and carrying them through this whole section for one quest isn't worth five slots.

            The desert drains your Hitpoints unless you wear desert clothing and carry water, so the first stop is the Shantay Pass shop.

            Start Prince Ali Rescue

            Walk south from Varrock to the Al Kharid toll gate and pay the 10 gp to pass.

            In the palace, talk to Hassan to start Prince Ali Rescue, then talk to Osman just outside. Hassan is in the palace's southern chamber; ask him whether he needs help, and he sends you to Osman, who waits outside the northern side of the palace. He lists what the rescue needs; you collect it in 1.4, so this is a dialogue stop that saves a later walk. The collecting happens in 1.4's Lumbridge and Draynor block, where you also smelt the bronze key yourself: since January 2026 Osman no longer makes it for you.

            Done when Prince Ali Rescue is started.

            Coins: about 25,000 gp · Slots: about 18/28 · Requirements: none

            Prepare for the desert at the Shantay Pass

            Walk south to the Shantay Pass. From Shantay's shop, buy a desert shirt, desert robe and desert boots and wear all three. They replace the clue hunter garb and boots, which you drop: Wintertodt is behind you, and both are dug up again for free if you ever want them. Destroy the rainbow scarf too, for the same reason: it has no stats, its only job was warm clothing for Wintertodt, and if you ever want it back Diango sells another for 120 gp and Gilbert, in the flower field south-east of Barbarian Village, gives one free. Then buy one full waterskin, one Shantay pass and three bronze bars: one for Tourist Trap's prototype dart and two spares, because the dart tip can fail. One waterskin and one pass are deliberate. A pass is needed for each entry into the desert, so once you are through the gate its slot is empty again, and the next paragraph explains why one skin is enough. The whole set costs little: 40 gp each for the shirt and robe, 20 gp for the boots, 30 gp for the waterskin, 5 gp for the pass and 8 gp a bar.

            How the heat works decides how much water you need. South of the pass, the desert hits you for 1 to 10 every 90 seconds by default; each piece of desert clothing stretches that interval, and a full waterskin takes the hit for you one dose at a time, four doses per skin. Towns, including Pollnivneach, have no heat at all, so the water only matters on the walks between them. When your skin runs dry, use your knife on a Kharidian cactus to fill it back to four doses. Inside the mining camp the heat stops altogether, and the bowl of water on the table in the camp's northern building refills the skin as well, so one skin and the knife carry you through the quest.

            Slots: about 22/28 · Requirements: none

            Done when you hold the desert kit.

            Complete Tourist Trap

            Talk to Irena at the Shantay Pass to start Tourist Trap. Her daughter Ana is being held at the Desert Mining Camp, and the quest needs 10 Fletching and 20 Smithing, both of which you have. Show a Shantay pass to the guard and cross into the desert.

            The quest's main fight is the Mercenary Captain at the mining camp gate. He can be safespotted with Water Strike or Water Bolt (Oziris has a video of the spot). He is level 47, and the order matters: talk to him first and finish his whole dialogue, because attacking him before it ends teleports you away, hurts you and costs you your water. The dialogue itself ends by starting the fight. The wiki's safespot uses one of his mercenaries: wait until one is close, talk to it so it stands in the captain's path, and cast from behind it. Take the metal key he drops. At the Bedabin Camp you smith a prototype dart from technical plans at Al Shabim's anvil, using one bronze bar and some of your feathers with your hammer. Both halves of the dart can fail, the smithing and the fletching, which is why spare bars matter more than spare feathers: you carry thousands of feathers. The two spare bronze bars from Shantay are for the tip: at about 29 Smithing it succeeds less than half the time, and a failure can destroy the bar. If all three go, Shantay's shop sells more at 8 gp each. At the end you smuggle Ana out of the camp in a barrel on the mine cart. Your knife slashes cacti for water if your waterskin runs dry. In the camp you swap clothes with a slave: the desert shirt and robe you're wearing go to him and you keep the slave robes, which give partial heat protection. The wiki's UIM note asks for two free slots on top of everything you take off at the gate (15 if a guard's dialogue goes wrong and you mine your way out), and talk to the Mercenary Captain before you attack him.

            UIM risk Don't drop the barrel while Ana is in it, and choose the mine cart driver's dialogue options with care: a wrong answer there can send you back through most of the quest, and paying him 100 gp is the wiki's alternative to the dialogue.

            The camp also checks what you wear. Before you go through the gate, take off everything except the desert clothing: the staff of air, the Chronicle, the clue hunter gloves and the forestry kit all go into your inventory.
