Count before you walk in: the Shantay pass was used up at the Shantay gate and the captain's metal key came in, so you hold about 22 items, and the four you take off make 26, which leaves the two free slots the wiki asks for. The four stay in your inventory until you are out of the camp for good; put them back on after the quest, the gloves above all, because The Feud needs them. Once inside, keep the slave robes on at all times: a guard who sees you without them sends you to the mine to work off 15 rocks. You dropped the pickaxe, so break a house teleport instead, which the wiki gives as the way out; a new Shantay pass is 5 gp if you come back in through the gate.

            UIM risk A guard who sees a weapon or armour on you throws you in a cell, and getting out costs time and can cost your waterskin.

            The reward is 2 quest points and two choices of 4,650 XP each, in Agility, Fletching, Smithing or Thieving (the same skill can be picked twice). Put both on Agility: 9,300 XP takes you from the 40 the rooftops left you on to about 42. Agility is still the slowest of the four for a UIM to train, and the barbarian fishing later in this section is planned from 43, which Death on the Isle's 7,500 Agility XP reaches just before it. Drop any spare bronze bars and quest leftovers, and wear the staff, the Chronicle, the gloves and the forestry kit again. The quest also leaves you a full set of slave robes, and they are what you wear in the desert for the rest of this section.

            Slots: about 19/28 · Requirements: 10 Fletching, 20 Smithing

            Done when Tourist Trap is complete.

            Complete The Feud

            Back in Al Kharid, talk to Ali Morrisane in the market to start The Feud; it needs 30 Thieving, which you passed in Hosidius. Buy a Kharidian headpiece and a fake beard from him and combine them into a desert disguise. Pick up an empty bucket for the camel dung step if you don't have one. The disguise doesn't have to be worn all the time; you put it on for the mayor's villa later.

            Break a house teleport tablet: your house is now in Pollnivneach, so the portal drops you at the start of the quest. Buy three beers from Ali the Barman (2 gp each) and use them on Drunken Ali to get information, then work through the gangs. Drunken Ali is in the bar in the south-east of town; finish each conversation before you use the next beer.

            1. Talk to the gang members, buy two camel receipts for 500 gp and deliver one to each gang.
            2. Join Ali the Operator's gang by pickpocketing three villagers. The third has to be knocked out with a blackjack first; for the second villager, pay a street urchin 10 gp to distract a villager.
            3. Steal the mayor's wife's jewels from the safe in the villa; the combination is 1, 1, 2, 3, 5, 8. Give them to Ali the Operator and find out who the traitor is.
            4. Put a coin in the snake charmer's money pot for a snake charm and basket, charm a desert snake for Ali the Hag, and buy red hot sauce from Ali the Kebab seller, use it on the camel food trough, and use your bucket on the brown dung that appears (repeat the sauce if it comes out green) for Ugthanki dung. Ali the Hag turns them into poison; put it in the traitor's beer.
            5. Defeat the two gang leaders' champions. The Menaphite Leader summons the level 75 Tough Guy: safespot him from behind the table inside the Menaphite tent. The Bandit Leader summons the level 70 bandit champion: safespot him from the house east of the leader, next to the table and chair. Kill the Tough Guy first, because he drops a willow blackjack, and the bandit champion only drops his adamant scimitar if you're carrying a willow blackjack.
            6. Talk to the mayor, then return to Ali Morrisane to complete the quest.

            A few of these steps have traps the list leaves out. Ali the Operator's tent is the purple one in the south-west of town, and your first pickpocket fails until you have spoken to him. For the second, make sure the urchin is next to a villager when you pay, then pickpocket the villager with the flashing arrow from behind; if it fails, talk to the urchin again and he retries without asking for more money. At the villa, hide behind the large cactus outside until the coast is clear, and keep one slot free: the safe is behind the landscape painting beside the bed, and the jewels need that slot. The snake charmer sits behind the pub, the desert snakes are north of Ali the Operator's tent, and you use the charm on a snake rather than playing it. Ali the Hag lives on the hill west of town. The Bandit Leader is in the north-west, by the rug merchant, and the mayor is at the well in the middle of town.

            The Feud asks you to wear gloves for one of its steps. Most gloves work, but not ice gloves, barrows gloves, vambraces or slayer gloves; the wiki lists the ones that do, and leather gloves always work if your clue hunter gloves aren't on the list. The step is the mayor's villa, so have the gloves and the disguise on before you go in.

            The reward is 15,000 Thieving XP, 500 gp and an oak blackjack, plus the willow blackjack and adamant scimitar from the champions. Keep the willow blackjack and drop the oak one; both blackjack the same bandits. Drop the desert disguise and the bucket too. Wield the adamant scimitar only if your Attack is high enough, otherwise drop it. The willow blackjack stays with you into 1.3, where blackjacking resumes with the rogue outfit.

            Done when The Feud is complete.

            Coins: about 24,500 gp · Slots: about 20/28 · Requirements: 30 Thieving to start; able to safespot two level 70+ champions

            Blackjack bandits to 55 Thieving

            The Feud has taken you to about 45 Thieving, which is enough for the bearded Pollnivnian bandits (45 to 55). Wield the willow blackjack. Lure a bandit into a quiet corner, knock it out, then pickpocket it twice while it's unconscious; repeat. If a knock-out fails, the bandit turns aggressive: try the knock-out again at once, or, with a full inventory, select Pickpocket on it, which steals nothing but resets its aggression (if it attacks anyway, unequip the blackjack and knock it out to stop the attacks).

            The bearded bandits wander the north of town, between the general store and the rug merchant. The wiki's luring spot is Ali the Dyer's house in the north: it is small, other bandits can't see into it, and its ladder resets aggression quickly. A bandit that is stuck in a spot like that stays there while you keep blackjacking it instead of walking back to its spawn. Your rhythm is right when an XP drop comes every two ticks, and each successful pickpocket is 65 Thieving XP and 40 gp.

            Buy wine from Faisal the Barman (Ali the Barman before The Feud) in the Asp & Snake bar as your food. Wine heals 11 and the empty jugs pile up in your inventory, one slot each, so drop them every time you buy more. The bar is in the south of town, sells wine for 1 gp and holds 13 jugs, restocking every 90 seconds, so buy a batch at a time rather than one jug per trip. The fail rate is high at these levels; it drops noticeably above 55.

            Stop at 55 Thieving, about 1,000 successful pickpockets past 50. Blackjacking past 55 without the rogue outfit wastes half the coins you could be making, and the outfit needs your 1.3 armour case. This session pays for the roughly 8,600 steel arrowtips and the charter coming up, so don't stop earlier. 55 is also where the bearded bandits stop being your target: from 55 the bandits without beards are the ones to blackjack.

            Done when you are 55 Thieving with about 125,000 gp.

            Coins: about 125,000 gp · Slots: about 20/28 plus wines · Requirements: The Feud, a blackjack, 45 Thieving

            Wiki references Prince Ali Rescue Shantay Pass Tourist Trap The Feud Blackjacking Sleeping Giants

            Catherby, Seers and Hemenster

            This block takes Fishing to 35, the level Tempoross needs, finishes Fletching to 46, and completes Murder Mystery and Fishing Contest. It is a low-risk, low-slot block: drop your remaining wines before you leave Pollnivneach, and the quest items here come and go within the same short walk. Fly fishing is the backbone, and both quests are threaded into the fishing rather than done as separate trips. Fishing Contest is already started: Austri gave you the fishing pass in the Taverley block, so the pass is the only thing it brings in. Nothing in this chapter needs Murder Mystery, but Chapter 2 does, for Recipe for Disaster's Lumbridge Guide and for King's Ransom, and this walk passes the mansion anyway.

            Gate in about 1,600 gp for the charter and about 52,000 gp of arrowtips; 20 Fishing for trout.
            Carry in about 19 to 21/28; drop the wines in Pollnivneach first.
            Exit 46 Fletching; 35 Fishing; Murder Mystery and Fishing Contest done; about 71,000 gp.
            Carries forward nothing new.

            Travel to Catherby and finish 46 Fletching

            Use the Fishing Trawler minigame teleport to reach Port Khazard and charter a ship to Catherby. The trader crew stand on the Port Khazard docks, and the ship drops you in the harbour south of Catherby; Hickton's Archery Emporium is in the centre of the town, a short walk north. In Hickton's Archery Emporium, buy steel arrowtips. If you made arrow shafts in Lumbridge, add a feather to each shaft to make headless arrows as you walk, then add a steel arrowtip to each headless arrow. Both actions work in batches of 15, and steel arrows need 30 Fletching, which you already have. Each steel arrow is about 6 Fletching XP (1 for the headless arrow, 5 for the tip). From about 32 Fletching, 46 (67,983 XP) needs roughly 51,500 XP, which is about 8,600 steel arrows: about 52,000 gp of arrowtips at Hickton's 6 gp (stock is 600 per world, so hop worlds between purchases), about 8,500 feathers (the 1,500 plus the 7,000 bought at Gerrant's; the oak logs' own XP covers the rest) and about 7,000 more shafts than you made in Lumbridge, roughly 235 oak logs. Check your Fletching level and recompute the arrow count before you buy. Keep going until 46 Fletching, buying more arrowtips as you need them; when you run out of shafts, chop oak logs north of Catherby with your steel axe and fletch them into shafts with your knife. Each oak log gives 30 shafts and 10 Fletching XP of its own, so the shafts you cut here bring the final arrow count down a little; recompute as you go rather than buying the full estimate up front. The charter to Catherby costs 1,600 gp.

            The steel arrows stack in one slot. They cost nothing to carry through 1.2, but nothing in Chapter 1 fires them, so drop them when 1.3 starts.

            Harry's Fishing Shop in Catherby doesn't sell fly fishing rods or feathers, which is why both were bought at Gerrant's in Port Sarim. Use the rod you brought from there.

            UIM risk Harry's, in the east of the town, stocks only the ordinary fishing rod, nets, harpoons, lobster pots and bait, so don't drop the fly fishing rod or the feathers before the Seers' fishing is done: Harry can't replace either.

            Slots: about 19/28 · Requirements: none

            Done when you are 46 Fletching.

            Collect the Fishing Contest supplies

            Walk west to Seers' Village. Take the garlic from the table in the house south-west of the Seers' Village bank. Walk west to McGrubor's Wood, which lies west of Seers' Village and north of Hemenster, and squeeze through the loose railing on its northern edge. The main entrance is closed to you (a forester stands in it and the southern gate is locked), so the railing is the only way in: the loose section sits immediately north-east of the small building set into the northern fence. The guard dogs inside are level 44 and aggressive, and they attack anyone who gets close, so move straight to the red vines west of the fairy ring, near the western fence. You dropped your wines in Pollnivneach and carry no food, but the wiki notes you can run past the dogs taking only a few hits, so don't stop to fight them. Check the vines with your spade until you have three red vine worms, then leave through the railing again. The wiki asks for at least two worms; the third is a spare that saves a second run past the dogs.

            Slots: about 21/28 · Requirements: spade

            Done when you hold the Fishing Contest supplies.

            Fly fish to 35 and complete Murder Mystery

            Fly fish with your rod and feathers at the river just north of the Seers' Village bank. Trout give 50 XP and need 20 Fishing; from 30 you also catch salmon at 70 XP. Drop every fish as it lands. Cooking comes from barbarian fishing later at no slot cost, so there's no reason to carry raw fish here.

            Between inventories, do Murder Mystery at the Sinclair Mansion just north of the fishing spot. You need an empty pot: buy one for 1 gp at Arhein's General Goods on the Catherby pier before you walk over. Arhein's is on the docks where your charter landed, so buy the pot as you arrive rather than walking back from Seers'. The mansion stands north of Camelot Castle. Talk to the guard at the gate and choose "Sure, I'll help" to start the quest. Investigate the sacks in the gardener's shed north-west of the mansion for flypaper; the wiki's quick guide recommends four, but you only need one for the dagger and one for each suspect you test, so three is enough. Inside, take the pungent pot and the criminal's dagger from the crime scene on the east side of the ground floor, and investigate the smashed window there for the criminal's thread. The thread's colour tells you which two suspects to check (red: Bob and Carol; green: Anna and David; blue: Elizabeth and Frank): search the barrels in their rooms for their silver items. Fill your empty pot with flour from the barrel in the kitchen, in the north-west corner of the mansion, dust the dagger and lift its print with flypaper, then dust each silver item, lift its print and use it on the dagger's unknown print until one matches. Drop each silver item once its print is lifted; the wiki confirms they aren't needed after the match. Then talk to the poison salesman in the pub west of the Seers' Village bank, ask the matching suspect what their poison was for, and search that spot to catch the lie. The salesman also has a Fremennik Trials line, so pick the Murder Mystery option when he offers both. Each suspect names a different spot: the compost heap for Anna, the beehive in the cow pen for Bob, the drain west of the front door for Carol, the spiders' nest upstairs between the two three-bed rooms for David, the fountain for Elizabeth and the family crest east of the front door for Frank. The liar's spot won't fit the story, and that is the clue you show the guard. Show the guard the matching print, the poison story and the thread to complete the quest, for 3 quest points, 1,406 Crafting XP and 2,000 gp. The wiki recommends 16 free slots; worked in this order the peak is about eight quest items, so if you're short, collect the Fishing Contest garlic and worms after this quest instead of before.

            Stop fishing at 35 Fishing. Drop the fly fishing rod; barbarian fishing later uses Otto's heavy rod instead. Keep the feather stack, though: the barbarian rod uses feathers as bait, and that grind is what the stack was sized for. The fly fishing rod is no use at Hemenster either: the wiki lists only an ordinary fishing rod or a pearl fishing rod for the contest, and Grandpa Jack sells the ordinary one next.

            Slots: about 21/28 · Requirements: 20 Fishing for trout

            Done when you are 35 Fishing and Murder Mystery is complete.

            Complete Fishing Contest at Hemenster

            Walk south-west from Seers' Village to Hemenster, west of the Ranging Guild. In the house opposite the entrance, buy a fishing rod from Grandpa Jack for 5 gp. Show your fishing pass to Morris at the gate and walk in. Morris lets you in only with the pass, so keep it until you are through the gate.

            Put the garlic into the third pipe from the gate on the south wall of the northern building. Do this before you pay: the Sinister Stranger, who is fishing at the spot by the pipes, complains about the smell once the contest starts and swaps places with you. Pay Bonzo the 5 gp entry fee, then fish at the spot by the pipes using the red vine worms. You catch a giant carp, the winning fish, and Bonzo hands you the fishing trophy. The giant carp exists only in this contest and comes from that spot with the worms, so fish nowhere else. Drop the fishing rod. If you lose the trophy before the hand-in, go back to Bonzo: the wiki's drop trick shows that he hands out another one.

            Walk north-east to Vestri at the western entrance of the White Wolf Mountain tunnel, above Catherby, and show him the trophy to complete Fishing Contest, for 2,437 Fishing XP and permanent access to the dwarves' tunnel. Vestri stands by the stairs down into the tunnel. The trophy has no other use; after the quest, using spare trophies on either dwarf just takes them from you, so drop any extra.

            Done when Fishing Contest is complete.

            Coins: about 71,000 gp · Slots: about 20/28 · Requirements: 10 Fishing

            Wiki references Hickton's Archery Emporium Steel arrow Murder Mystery Fishing Contest McGrubor's Wood

            Tempoross to 56 Fishing

            This block trains Fishing from 35 to 56 at Tempoross and banks a large stack of reward permits in its reward pool. Tempoross suits a UIM almost perfectly. The crates on the docks supply the harpoons, ropes, buckets and hammers the fight uses, so you arrive with an empty-ish inventory and leave with nothing new in it. It is also safe in the way a UIM cares about most: Tempoross's attacks deal no damage, and the only things the cove can take from you are its own tools and the harpoonfish you are holding, so nothing from your kit is ever at risk here. The reward pool is the third deferred storage of the chapter: it holds up to 8,000 permits, and each reward is rolled at the moment you collect it, using your Fishing level then. Ultimate Ironmen used to be capped at 10 stored permits; that cap was raised to the same 8,000 as everyone else.

            Gate in 35 Fishing.
            Carry in about 17/28: the crates on the docks supply the tools.
            Exit 56 Fishing; a stack of reward permits left in the pool.
            Carries forward the Tempoross reward pool (opened in 1.3).

            Before you go, trim the inventory down to the core kit: coins, research notes, spade, hammer, knife, house teleports, steel bars, the steel arrows, your five rune stacks (6 air, 6 earth, water, mind and chaos), the staff of air, the steel axe, the feather stack and the marks of grace, with the willow blackjack wielded and the Chronicle worn. Drop the waterskin and the fishing pass. That's about 17 slots, which leaves about 11 for harpoonfish.

            Travel to the Ruins of Unkah

            Cast Lumbridge Home Teleport, walk east through the Al Kharid toll gate (10 gp) and south through the town to Ferryman Sathwood, just south of the Al Kharid bank. He ferries you to the Ruins of Unkah and the Sea Spirit dock. Talk to Captain Pudi on the dock the first time for an outline of the fight. The boat out to the fight leaves from the sea spirit dock on the west side of the ruins.

            Slots: about 17/28 · Requirements: 35 Fishing

            Done when you are at Tempoross.

            Fight Tempoross on a mass world

            Join a Tempoross mass world, where a full boat makes the fight fast and forgiving. The wiki lists worlds 341 (UK), 422 (US west) and 463 (Germany) as the official Tempoross worlds. Take a harpoon, a rope, a bucket and a hammer from the crates as the round starts; they belong to the minigame and stay behind when you leave. Get off the boat quickly at the start; the wiki's tip is to click far west on the minimap. Each round, harpoon raw harpoonfish at the fishing spots on your side of the island, cook them at the shrine, and load them into the cannon. On a mass world, load only the cannon nearest the ship's entrance, as the wiki advises. Below 85 Fishing the wiki's standard rhythm is to catch about seven fish, cook them, and load them; a double fishing spot usually appears around then, and it is always worth moving to. When Tempoross goes down, run to the end of your dock and harpoon the spirit pool until he recovers. When a wave is announced, tether yourself to the nearest mast or totem pole with the rope, or it knocks you down and you lose the fish you're holding. You usually have time to catch one or two more fish first. Waves can break masts and totems, and you can't tether to a broken one, so repair it with the hammer before the next wave. Douse fires with a bucket of water and repair broken masts with the hammer when you're near them. Put fires out early: they spread, and running through one destroys the minigame tools you carry.

            UIM risk If the storm intensity reaches 100% before Tempoross's energy is gone, the encounter fails and the round's points are lost.
            UIM risk Nothing else is lost, so take the boat out again.

            Cooking the harpoonfish before loading them gives more points and Cooking XP. A cooked fish loaded into the cannon is worth 65 points against 20 for a raw one; the trade-off is that skipping the cooking gives noticeably more Fishing XP per hour (the wiki's example at 35 Fishing is about 30,000 against 22,000). This guide takes the points, because the permits are what feed 1.3. Keep going until 56 Fishing; from 35 that is around 160,000 XP and a few comfortable hours on a mass world. The easy and medium Tempoross combat achievements come naturally along the way.

            Slots: about 17/28 between rounds · Requirements: 35 Fishing

            Done when you are 56 Fishing.

            Leave the permits in the reward pool. Don't collect your reward permits yet. The pool's loot includes planks, oak planks, steel nails, raw fish, caskets, feathers, spirit flakes and Tempoross's uniques, including the tackle box, a storage item that holds your fishing gear. All of it is rolled with your Fishing level at collection, so don't cash permits in before 50 Fishing. The planks and oak planks you dropped at the start of this section come back from this pool in 1.3, exactly when 1.3 needs them.

            Target: 56 Fishing, not a permit count. The pool's loot is random, so no number of permits guarantees a number of planks; leave when you reach 56. Play for points anyway: a game pays 1 permit at 2,000 points and 1 more for every 700 points after that, with a chance at an extra one from the remainder, so aim for at least 4,000 points a game (about 4 permits): catch, cook and load fish all round instead of standing at the cannon, and always harpoon the spirit pool.

            What to expect: the pool rolls each item at a fixed rate per permit (the fish table, 45/80, adds up exactly to the listed fish rarities, so the rates are per permit). At about 72 Fishing when you collect in 1.3 (the same 50 to 75 fish band as at 56: you wait because the pool holds the rolls until 1.3 needs them, not for better fish), as a reference, 200 permits come to roughly 220 noted planks (7/160 per permit, 20–30 each), 125 noted oak planks (1/32, 15–25), 1,000 steel nails (1/80, 300–500), 125 raw swordfish and 340 raw lobster, about ten caskets and a 39% chance at the tackle box. The planks and oak planks are worth about 14,000 Construction XP as oak larders and wooden bookcases, and the swordfish are 1.3's food. With fewer permits, or unlucky rolls, the Construction block takes what the pool gave and tops up with sawmill planks.

            Take the ferry back to Al Kharid.

            Slots: about 17/28 · Requirements: none

            Wiki references Tempoross Tempoross strategies Reward pool (Tempoross) Ruins of Unkah

            Fishing Trawler and the angler's outfit

            This block collects the four-piece angler's outfit, which adds 2.5% Fishing XP for the whole barbarian fishing grind that follows and is the base of the spirit angler's outfit later. It is the most passive block of the section. The full set takes about 48 games on average, so plan it for a relaxed evening.

            Gate in 15 Fishing.
            Carry in about 19/28 with the slave robes.
            Exit the four-piece angler's outfit.
            Carries forward the angler's outfit (into the armour case in 1.3).

            You need only two tools beyond your core kit: the steel axe and the hammer you already carry. An axe and a hammer let you skip swamp paste entirely, which saves a purchase and a slot. The axe has to be an ordinary one (a blessed axe doesn't chop the tentacles), and your steel axe is fine; how often a chop succeeds depends on the axe and your Woodcutting level. The minigame itself is harmless: the wiki describes no way to die on the trawler, and if the boat sinks the game just ends and you swim back to shore for 2 damage.

            Play Fishing Trawler

            Use the Fishing Trawler minigame teleport to reach Port Khazard; Fishing Trawler needs 15 Fishing. Board the trawler with your axe and hammer. A game takes about 6.5 minutes with docking and cutscenes, 5 of them trawling. The wiki lists worlds 315 (US west) and 370 (US east) as the official Trawler worlds. During a game, the boat springs leaks and tentacles grab the railings. Chop the tentacles with your axe and repair the broken railings with your hammer, staying close to the railings and clicking quickly, because the other players compete for them. If you want extra activity, bail water with a bailing bucket; an ordinary bucket doesn't work, but you can search the crates on the trawler's deck for a bailing bucket.

            Your contribution meter never drops; once it reaches 50 you are guaranteed a reward roll, so from then on you can relax until the end of the game. Points above 50 add only fish, not better outfit odds, and you drop the fish anyway. Collect your reward from the net at the end of each game; it is on the Port Khazard dock, west of the boat. Each reward roll has a 1 in 12 chance of a piece you don't own yet, and pieces you already own never come up, so you can't waste rolls on duplicates. Against the 48-game average, about half of players have the set within 44 games. Keep every angler piece and wear it (each piece needs 34 Fishing, which Tempoross has taken you well past): the hat, top, waders and boots go in your head, body, legs and feet slots. Tourist Trap took your desert shirt and robe (you handed them to the slave), so wear the angler pieces over the slave robes; for the desert at the end of this section, wear the slave robes (partial heat protection) or buy a new desert set at the Shantay Pass. Drop all the fish.

            If you get a rusty sword, have Tindel Marchant in Port Khazard identify it for 100 gp; keep it only if it's an upgrade you can wield. He stands on the southern dock. Having him identify one is also an easy Ardougne Diary task, so it is worth the 100 gp even if you never wield the sword. Most of the time he hands back a bronze to mithril sword or scimitar; about one time in 100 he keeps it as an artefact and pays you roughly 2,000 gp instead, which doesn't count for the diary task.

            Stop when you have all four angler pieces. Only the full set gives the extra 0.5% on top of the pieces' own 2%, so don't stop at three. You wear or carry the outfit until 1.3, where the oak armour case at 46 Construction becomes its permanent home.

            Slots: about 19/28 with the slave robes in the inventory · Requirements: 15 Fishing

            Done when you own the four-piece angler's outfit.
            Wiki references Fishing Trawler Angler's outfit Tindel Marchant

            Waterfall, Fight Arena and Tree Gnome Village

            This block completes three combat quests south and west of Ardougne. Together they give about 37,000 Attack XP and 13,750 Strength XP, which takes Attack to around 40 and Strength to around 30, plus the 15 Strength that barbarian fishing needs and the spirit tree network. Every fight is safespotted with Water Strike or Water Bolt from your staff of air, so you never melee anything here. The order follows the geography and the item chain: Lady Servil stands at the corner of the Tree Gnome Village maze, the dungeon under that maze holds Glarial's pebble, and the Waterfall Quest can't start its tomb step without the pebble, so it comes last.

            Gate in safespots for every fight; 5 free slots for the Waterfall reward.
            Carry in about 17 to 19/28.
            Exit Fight Arena, Tree Gnome Village and the Waterfall Quest done; about 40 Attack and 30 Strength; about 72,000 gp.
            Carries forward two cut diamonds and two gold bars.

            Two UIM traps sit in this block, and this block is built around both. In Fight Arena, dying to General Khazard moves your whole inventory to a deathpile outside the instance, so you leave through the door instead of fighting him. And Glarial's Tomb bans weapons (axes and pickaxes included), armour, runes, a knife, needle, thread, wool, leather and nails, logs, feathers, shafts and arrows, clue scrolls, skillcapes and the Ardougne cloak, magic secateurs and the looting bag (a rune pouch only if it's empty), so you put those on a table outside, walk the short tomb, and pick them up again.

            Prepare in East Ardougne

            From Port Khazard, walk north to East Ardougne. At Aemad's Adventuring Supplies, buy one rope for the Waterfall. That rope is the only new item this block needs from a shop. Swap your angler's outfit back into the inventory and put the slave robes on, or keep wearing the angler pieces; neither set matters for these fights. The head and body pieces of whichever set you wear come off for Fight Arena's Khazard armour, and everything worn goes on the table for Glarial's Tomb, which bans armour.

            Slots: about 17/28 · Requirements: none

            Done when your kit for the three quests is ready.

            Complete Fight Arena

            Walk south-west of the Ardougne Monastery to Lady Servil, by the north-east corner of the Tree Gnome Village maze, and talk to her to start Fight Arena. Search the chest in the most north-eastern house of the Khazard camp for the Khazard helmet and armour, and wear both; your current helmet and body piece go into the inventory for the duration. The camp is the group of buildings south of the monastery, and the prison is south of the camp. The guards let you into the prison only while you wear the armour, and they won't speak to you inside if you take it off, so keep both pieces on until the fights are over. Your other slots don't matter, so your staff stays wielded.

            Talk to Sammy Servil in his cell, the northernmost cell of the east wing, and ask where the keys are; he points you to the Head Guard by the staircase in the south-east corner of the jail, who mentions that he wants a Khali brew. In the Khazard bar, west of the jail's western entrance, buy a Khali brew for 5 gp and give it to the Head Guard. If the barman doesn't offer the brew, finish the Head Guard's dialogue first: the purchase option appears only after that. He passes out and you take his keys. Use them on Sammy Servil's cell, the most north-eastern one, and you are pulled into the arena.

            1. Khazard ogre (level 63). Lure it behind the skeleton near the arena wall so it can't reach you, and kill it with Water Strike or Water Bolt from there. If the fight doesn't start, talk to Justin Servil; don't do this once the ogre is already out, because talking to him then brings a second one.
            2. Back in your cell, talk to your cellmate Hengrad to start the next fight.
            3. Khazard scorpion (level 44). It reaches you quickly, so run straight to the same skeleton safespot as the fight starts. It isn't poisonous.
            4. Bouncer (level 137). He is a melee-only hellhound; use the same skeleton safespot and keep casting.
            5. General Khazard. Don't fight him. He is optional and the quest completes either way, and a death in the arena drops your items in a deathpile outside the instance, so don't risk it. Walk out through the door in the south-east corner of the arena instead, using its Quick-escape option.

            If a fight goes badly before Khazard, teleport out rather than risk the death: teleporting is allowed during the fights, and the Khazard Guard by the prison door puts you back into the arena when you return.

            Return to Lady Servil to complete the quest, for 12,175 Attack XP, 2,175 Thieving XP and 1,000 gp. The quest also gives 2 quest points and lets you keep the Khazard armour. Take off the Khazard armour, put your own gear back on, and drop the Khazard pieces.

            Done when Fight Arena is complete.

            Coins: about 72,000 gp · Slots: about 17/28 · Requirements: none; safespot every fight

            Complete Tree Gnome Village and get Glarial's pebble

            Walk into the Tree Gnome Village maze and follow it to the centre, then talk to King Bolren to start Tree Gnome Village. Elkoy leads you back out. Chop six normal logs near the maze with your steel axe; they are the quest's only item.

            Walk north to the battlefield and talk to Commander Montai, in red, at the south-west edge of the battlefield south of the river. Give him the six logs, then talk to him again. Find the three tracker gnomes on the battlefield and ask each one for his coordinate. The first is north-west of the Khazard stronghold, by its largest north-western building; the second is locked in a prison cell in the north-eastern building; the third wanders outside its south-west corner. Then fire the ballista in the south-west corner of the battlefield, past the bridge (not the catapult by Montai). If the shot misses, fire again. The third tracker speaks in riddles: "less than my hands" is 0001, "more than my head, less than my fingers" 0002, "more than we, less than our feet" 0003, and "my legs and your legs" 0004. Climb over the crumbled wall into the Khazard stronghold and take the orb of protection from the chest. The wall is at the building where the first tracker stood. Level 48 Khazard commanders attack you inside, and the wiki warns of an ambush just past the wall; you can kill them or ignore them. The chest is up the ladder in the western room. Come back down and leave through the nearby door. Back at the village, talk to Elkoy and he takes you to the centre of the maze again.

            Back in the village, Bolren tells you the other two orbs were taken by the Khazard warlord (level 112) in the north-west of the battlefield. He stands outside the West Ardougne wall, past the wolves, with two Khazard troopers near him, and he doesn't attack until you talk to him. The orbs drop only if you have already heard this from Bolren, so don't go after the warlord early. Start the fight, then walk west until he stops chasing you near a large dead tree, step a little south, and cast from outside his range until he dies. Don't let the fight drag on, or he can despawn. Pick up the two orbs and return to Bolren to complete the quest, for 11,450 Attack XP, a gnome amulet and the spirit tree network. It also gives 2 quest points. The spirit trees matter in 1.4, where you ride the one at the Grand Exchange to and from the Tree Gnome Stronghold for The Grand Tree and later trips.

            While you're in the village, climb down into the dungeon under it. Take the key from the crate, open the gate, and talk to Golrie inside to get Glarial's pebble for the Waterfall Quest. The entrance is in the maze just south of the main village. The key is in the large crate in the south-east corner of the eastern room, the one coloured differently from the others; the eastern room also has giant bats and zombies. Golrie is locked behind a door in the western room, which is full of level 28 hobgoblins, so open it and talk to him without stopping. If you lose the pebble later, Golrie gives you another, so there's no reason to carry two. Drop the gnome amulet unless you want it.

            Slots: about 18/28 with the pebble and rope · Requirements: none; safespot the warlord

            Done when Tree Gnome Village is complete and you hold the pebble.

            Complete the Waterfall Quest

            Walk north-west to Almera's house above Baxtorian Falls and talk to her to start Waterfall Quest. Take the raft down to Hudon on the rock in the river and talk to him; you wash ashore. Go through every line of Hudon's dialogue before you leave him: if you cut it short, the book you need next doesn't appear. From his island, swim south and you wash up near a large building with a fisherman outside. In Hadley's house nearby, read the book on Baxtorian, which tells you about Glarial's tomb. The book is upstairs, in the southernmost bookcase on the eastern wall. Read it and then close it.

            Glarial's tomb is east of Hadley's house. You may not take weapons, armour, runes, fletching items or a looting bag inside; a rune pouch is allowed only if empty. The wiki's UIM advice for players without storage is to leave the banned items on a table at the nearby Tourist Information Centre. That is Hadley's house, where you just read the book, so you are already standing next to the table. Jewellery is allowed, so it can stay on; if you are unsure about a worn item, put it on the table too. Do this on a quiet world. Put your staff, steel axe, knife, worn armour, all rune stacks, the feather and arrow stacks and anything else from that list on the table. Keep your coins on you: the wiki allows them in the tomb. Items left on a table disappear after about 10 minutes and other players can see them after 60 seconds, so don't stop on the way. The tomb also holds level 84 moss guardians between the entrance and the amulet chest; keep two free slots for the amulet and urn, and walk past them with your hits in mind, and eat if needed. The tomb is single-way combat, so the wiki's advice for low levels is to start a fight with one of the skeletons as you go in: while you are fighting it, the moss guardians, which hit much harder, can't attack you. Use Glarial's pebble on the tombstone, climb down, search the chest for Glarial's amulet and Glarial's coffin for Glarial's urn, climb back out, and pick everything up from the table again. The chest is on the west side and the coffin on the south, and you can be attacked while you search the coffin, so search it and leave by the ladder straight away.

            For the final part you need the rope, 6 air, 6 water and 6 earth runes, Glarial's amulet and urn, and 5 free inventory slots for the reward. Take the raft from Almera's house again. Use your rope on the rock, then on the dead tree, to reach the ledge, and enter the waterfall dungeon. When the raft crashes, don't swim or touch the tree before the rope is in place; the rock is the grey one downstream. Search the northernmost crates on the right-hand path for the key to the next door, and don't climb into the barrel next to the raft. The dungeon has fire giants and other aggressive monsters that hit hard for your level, so keep moving toward the door you need. The fire giants are level 86. With the key, go back to the left-hand path, keep clear of the fire giants and open the locked door.

            In the final room, use one air, one water and one earth rune on each of the six pillars. Work round the pillars carefully: a wrong placement can wash you out of the dungeon or hit you for up to 20, and a rune of a type already on a pillar is refused with a message. Before you go in, check you hold nothing you would drop in this room, because items dropped inside it can't be picked up again once you use the amulet. Use Glarial's amulet on the statue of Glarial, and the floor rises. Then use Glarial's urn on the Chalice of Eternity and finish the dialogue. Don't click "Take treasure" on the chalice: that washes you out of the dungeon. Being washed out after the amulet is on the statue is expensive: the wiki says you then need another amulet to get back in, which means another trip to the tomb and the table. The quest completes, for 1 quest point, 13,750 Attack and 13,750 Strength XP, 2 cut diamonds, 2 gold bars and 40 mithril seeds. Keep the diamonds and gold bars for now (1.3 sells them for coins before the Mage Training Arena); drop the mithril seeds unless you want them.

            Done when the Waterfall Quest is complete.

            Coins: about 72,000 gp · Slots: about 19/28 · Requirements: 5 free slots for the reward

            Wiki references Fight Arena Tree Gnome Village Waterfall Quest Ultimate Ironman Guide/Quests Aemad's Adventuring Supplies

            Barbarian fishing and Death on the Isle

            This block is the long grind of the section: barbarian fishing from about 43 to about 53 Agility, which also takes Fishing into the 70s, Strength up, and Cooking past 43 if you cut-eat. It is UIM-friendly in the best way. It needs only Otto's heavy rod, your feather stack and your knife, everything you catch is cut or dropped on the spot, and nothing leaves the grotto with you. Death on the Isle comes first, as soon as you have the rod: the rooftops and Tourist Trap's lamps have already taken you past its 32 Agility, and its 7,500 Agility XP and 10,000 Thieving XP are worth taking as early as possible.

            Gate in 48 Fishing, 15 Agility and 15 Strength; 32 Agility, 34 Thieving and Children of the Sun for Death on the Isle.
            Carry in the barbarian rod and feathers: about 20 to 22/28.
            Exit about 53 Agility, Fishing in the 70s; Death on the Isle done; about 67,000 gp.
            Carries forward nothing new.

            Unlock barbarian fishing with Otto

            Walk from Port Khazard or Seers' Village to the Barbarian Outpost first and do the Barbarian Assault tutorial with Captain Cain: the minigame teleport only works once that tutorial is complete or you have chosen to skip it, and this chapter uses it twice (the barcrawl start and the walk to Rellekka). The teleport lands at the Barbarian Outpost and shares the 20-minute minigame cooldown. Then walk to Otto's Grotto, by the river above Baxtorian Falls. Talk to Otto Godblessed and ask about barbarian training, then choose fishing. In the dialogue, answer "You think so?" to begin the training, then ask "What can I learn about the use of a fishing rod?" for the fishing part. Barbarian fishing needs 48 Fishing, 15 Agility and 15 Strength; Tempoross, Tourist Trap and the Waterfall Quest have given you all three. Search the bed in Otto's grotto for the barbarian rod, then fish one leaping fish at the spots just outside to complete that part of his training. The rod works only at these spots and at Mount Quidamortem in Kourend, where Chapter 2 does its own long barbarian fishing block.

            Slots: about 20/28 with the barbarian rod · Requirements: 48 Fishing, 15 Agility, 15 Strength

            Done when you hold the barbarian rod.

            Complete Death on the Isle

            Death on the Isle needs 32 Agility and 34 Thieving, neither boostable, plus Children of the Sun. Use the Fishing Trawler minigame teleport to reach Port Khazard and charter a ship to Aldarin. Leave the barbarian rod in your inventory for the trip; it is one slot, and Otto's bed gives you another if you lose it.

            Talk to Patzi by the entrance to Villa Lucens to start Death on the Isle. The quest is a murder mystery: you get into the villa in a servant's uniform from the house just west of the Aldarin General Store (reached through its southern window), investigate the cellar, pickpocket the suspects for their notes, and search the backstage crate, costume rack and bookshelf before accusing the culprits: Adala first (she stole the Principum Red), then Naiatli during the play. The quest gives 2 quest points, 10,000 Thieving, 7,500 Agility and 5,000 Crafting XP and the costume needle. Twice in the quest you fight a low-level enemy unarmed, and the Head Butler takes your items for safekeeping before you go in, so what you carry is safe with him. Talk to him to get everything back when you leave.

            UIM risk The wiki confirms you can't die in those unarmed fights, so neither of them can cost you your inventory.

            A few points keep the quest moving. Wait until the Wandering Guard has walked away before you climb through the southern window, and take the uniform from the wardrobe inside. Then go back to Patzi, follow Adala to the staff entrance and walk south along the hedge to the Head Butler. When you investigate the antique wine in the cellar, you are framed for the murder and taken away by the guards; that is scripted, so let it happen. Adala (level 49) stops fighting once either of you is close to death.

            The rewards are 10,000 Thieving XP, 7,500 Agility XP and 5,000 Crafting XP. Afterwards, talk to the costumer backstage for the costume needle. It acts as a needle and an unlimited supply of thread, so it replaces two items for every Crafting job in 1.3 and beyond, and costs one slot. A few jobs still want real thread, and the guide handles them where they come up: Elemental Workshop I's bellows in 1.3 and Creature of Fenkenstrain in 1.4 each buy their own. If you drop or lose the needle, the costumer backstage gives you another. Talk to the Head Butler too if you want the masks and the butler's tray, then drop them.

            Don't buy Blackbird red here: Hunter is reached through the bird runs themselves, and the +1 boost isn't worth the 300 gp each plus the Attack drain. Charter back to Port Khazard. The Aldarin charter costs 2,300 gp each way.

            Done when Death on the Isle is complete.

            Coins: about 67,000 gp · Slots: about 22/28 with the needle · Requirements: 32 Agility, 34 Thieving, Children of the Sun

            Barbarian fish to about 53 Agility

            Walk from Port Khazard or Seers' Village back to Otto's spots and fish the leaping fish spots outside the grotto with your feathers as bait, wearing the angler's outfit for 2.5% more Fishing XP. Each catch gives Fishing XP plus a little Agility and Strength XP. From 48 Fishing you catch leaping trout; from 58 Fishing, 30 Agility and 30 Strength you catch leaping salmon; and from 70 Fishing, 45 Agility and 45 Strength leaping sturgeon, each worth more Agility than the last.

            Cut-eat every fish: use your knife on it to cut it open. Only some cuts give roe or caviar (the chance rises with your Cooking level), which you eat straight away for Cooking XP, and then sometimes fish offcuts. Each cut gives 10 Cooking XP for a trout or salmon and 15 for a sturgeon, and a cut can fail. The offcuts work as bait, so they stretch your feather stack, but you get fewer than one per fish: on a successful cut, trout give one half the time, salmon three times in four and sturgeon five times in six. The rod always takes offcuts before feathers, so they never pile up while you still have feathers; each one saves a feather as it is used. If you don't cut-eat, drop each fish. Without cut-eating you end up training Cooking at the Hosidius mess hall to 70 later, so cut-eating is worth the extra click.

            Fish and cut-eat to about 53 Agility. That is about 85,000 Agility XP of fishing, the amount your feather stack was bought for, and on the way you pass 43 Cooking, which you want by the end of the section, while Fishing climbs into the 70s.

            If your feathers run out before then, keep fishing: offcuts work as bait like feathers, and once the feathers are gone each successful cut can still hand you the bait for the next catch, so the session tails off rather than stopping dead. You won't have a stored reserve of offcuts, because the rod has been using each one as it arrived. When you're done, drop the barbarian rod: Otto keeps a spare under his bed for whenever you come back.

            Slots: about 21/28 · Requirements: none

            Done when you are about 53 Agility.
            Wiki references Barbarian Training Barbarian Fishing Death on the Isle Costume needle

            Back to Pollnivneach

            The section closes where your house now stands. Death on the Isle has left Thieving at about 55 after the first blackjacking session, so there is no second session here. This block just returns you to your house in Pollnivneach and explains where your rune pouch comes from, because Last Man Standing, where most players get one, isn't the best way for a UIM.

            Gate in Death on the Isle done.
            Carry in about 22/28.
            Exit you are home in Pollnivneach, about 67,000 gp.
            Carries forward the plan for the rune pouch (1.3).

            Return to Pollnivneach

            Break a house teleport tablet and walk out of your portal in Pollnivneach. Pollnivneach is a town, so the desert heat doesn't drain you inside it; put the slave robes back on only for the walks outside, and move the angler's outfit into your inventory. Wield the willow blackjack. 1.3 opens right here with a short blackjacking session that pays for the costume room and the planks, so the blackjack goes straight back to work.

            You are at 55 Thieving. From here, every level you train without the rogue outfit earns half the coins it could, and the outfit comes in 1.3 with your armour case. Drop the empty wine jugs.

            Done when you are home in Pollnivneach.

            Coins: about 67,000 gp · Slots: about 22/28 · Requirements: none

            Looking ahead: the rune pouch. Last Man Standing points can buy a rune pouch, but points come only from Competitive games, which need 750 total level and 30 quest points (or 1,500 total level), and the pouch note then costs 75 points from Justine, who since May 2026 sells it only after you have won at least one game. Since August 2025 the Mage Training Arena's Rewards Guardian also sells a rune pouch for pizazz points, and 1.3 spends a whole block in that arena for Bones to Peaches anyway. So this guide takes the pouch there, with no PvP at all. The arena sells it only if you don't already own one. The pouch holds three rune types, up to 16,000 of each, in one slot, which is what lets 1.3 carry law, nature and water runes without three separate stacks.

            If you enjoy PvP and already have 750 total level and 30 quest points, Last Man Standing still works: the looting bag costs 1 point and the rune pouch note 75. Chapter 1 doesn't use a looting bag; for a UIM it is a Wilderness-only storage that you empty by dying or destroying it there; destroying it in Ferox Enclave or within two tiles of the ditch deletes its contents, and the costume room covers what this chapter needs.

            Requirements: none now; the Mage Training Arena block in 1.3 lists its own

            Wiki references Blackjacking Ultimate Ironman Guide/Thieving Last Man Standing Justine Rune pouch

            End of section 1.2

            You finish 1.2 in Pollnivneach with about 56 Fishing from Tempoross pushed into the 70s by barbarian fishing, about 53 Agility and about 30 marks of grace, 55 Thieving, 46 Fletching, about 67,000 gp and seventeen more quests done. Three deferred storages now hold your future loot: the Wintertodt Reward Cart, the Tempoross reward pool and Veos's lamps. 1.3 starts from this exact spot: a short blackjacking session pays for the house, and the Tempoross pool is collected straight after it, so nothing has to move before you begin.

            1.3: Money, quests and the road to Fossil Island

            This is the longest section of the chapter and the one where the account stops feeling like an early UIM. It builds four things the rest of the guide leans on, in this order: your first permanent storage (a costume room with an armour case, at 46 Construction), the rogue outfit, about 1.4 million gp of blackjacking money, and a rune pouch and Bones to Peaches from the Mage Training Arena. Around those it completes seventeen quests in regional sweeps, from The Dig Site to Bone Voyage, which opens Fossil Island. Each sweep finishes the quests it starts: a quest whose items would ride for weeks waits for 1.4 instead, so this section's inventory never relies on room it doesn't have.

            Nothing here starts from a banked 1.25 million gp, and nothing takes you into the Wilderness Mage Arena for runes. Money comes after the rogue outfit, because without it every blackjack earns half, and runes come from Ali Morrisane in Al Kharid, one step from the Mage Training Arena, so nothing you own ever enters the Wilderness. The rune pouch comes from the arena's own reward shop instead of Last Man Standing, so this section has no PvP at all.

            Three deaths in this section are planned, and each is written into its block: one in Falador Castle before the Rogues' Den, one in Falador Castle again before Entrana, and one to the rock crabs outside Rellekka before Peer the Seer. A UIM keeps nothing on death, so each one moves your entire inventory into a deathpile that lasts 60 logged-in minutes and that only you can see. No other death in this section is safe, so read the warnings in Shades of Mort'ton and Nature Spirit. Koschei's fight is safe.

            Funding the house

            You start 1.3 where 1.2 ended: in Pollnivneach, at 55 Thieving, with about 67,000 gp. The costume room (50,000 gp), the kitchen (5,000), about 680 sawmill oak planks (about 170,000) and two house moves in the next two blocks cost about 240,000 gp in total, so this short session pays for them. It is the only blackjacking you do without the rogue outfit, and it stops the moment the house is paid for.

            Gate in 55 Thieving for unbearded bandits.
            Carry in the 1.2 kit and wines: about 18/28 plus wines.
            Exit about 265,000 gp.
            Carries forward the coins for the costume room, kitchen, planks and two house moves.

            Your kit for the next three blocks is the 1.2 kit minus two items: drop the steel arrows (nothing in this chapter fires them) and keep the willow blackjack wielded. Take off the slave robes and drop them: Pollnivneach is a town, so the heat does not apply there, and a desert shirt, robe and boots cost 40, 40 and 20 gp at the Shantay Pass shop when you next cross the desert. Wear the angler's outfit instead of carrying it; it is the first thing that goes in the armour case.

            Blackjack to 265,000 gp

            At 55 Thieving the bearded bandits are no longer the best target. Move to the unbearded Pollnivnian bandits in the north of the town, between the general store and the rug merchant; the cycle is the same as before (knock out, pickpocket twice, retry the knock-out at once if one fails). Buy wine from Faisal the Barman as you need it and drop each empty jug immediately.

            The unbearded bandits carry steel scimitars instead of blackjacks, and each successful pickpocket on a knocked-out one pays 50 coins and 84.3 Thieving XP. Lure one into an empty house where no other bandit can see it. Ali the Dyer's small house in the north of the town is the usual spot, and its ladder lets you reset a bandit that has turned on you. Keep the inventory full with wine, so a mistimed click on a conscious bandit can't stun you; it costs you only the click.

            Stop at about 265,000 gp, which covers the house and leaves about 25,000, and the next block's casket coins bring that to about 35,000. You get there at about 65 Thieving. Don't chase a level: anything beyond the house is cheaper to earn once you wear the rogue outfit.

            Done when you hold about 265,000 gp.

            Coins: about 265,000 gp · Slots: about 18/28 plus wines · Requirements: 55 Thieving for unbearded bandits

            Wiki references Blackjacking Pollnivnian bandits

            Planks from the Tempoross pool

            Every permit you earned at Tempoross in 1.2 is still in the reward pool, and the pool rolls its loot at your Fishing level when you collect. At about 72 Fishing the fish band is salmon, tuna, lobster, bass and swordfish, and the non-fish table includes noted planks, noted oak planks and steel nails. About 200 permits give roughly 220 planks, 125 oak planks and 1,000 nails, about 14,000 Construction XP for the next block at no cost; the rolls are random, so buy sawmill planks for any shortfall.

            Gate in the Tempoross permits in the pool; about 72 Fishing.
            Carry in about 24/28 with the plank, nail, swordfish and seaweed stacks and the saw.
            Exit the pool collected; a saw; about 275,000 gp.
            Carries forward the plank stacks, the swordfish and the seaweed.

            Collect the reward pool

            The pool is not at Tempoross Cove itself: it sits on the Sea Spirit dock just west of the Ruins of Unkah, where you boarded the ship in 1.2, and no minigame teleport goes there. From Pollnivneach, take the magic carpet back to the Shantay Pass (200 gp until Rogue Trader is started later in this section, which halves the fare), walk north through Al Kharid to Ferryman Sathwood just south of the bank, and let him ferry you to the Ruins of Unkah, as you did in 1.2. Searching the pool needs a small or big fishing net in your inventory: take the free small net from the Spirit Angler and drop it after the last search: nothing else in this section fishes. Before you search, make sure you have at least eight free slots, because rewards arrive one every three ticks as separate stacks. The Big-search option takes five rolls per click, which saves clicking through a couple of hundred permits one at a time.

            Keep the noted planks, noted oak planks and steel nails for Construction. Keep the noted raw swordfish: it is your food for the rest of the section (unnote a handful at a bank booth and cook them, 45 Cooking). Drop the lobster, salmon, tuna and bass stacks: Caleb's fish for Family Crest are caught next to him in Catherby when that quest is done in 1.4, and every extra stack costs a slot through the Mage Training Arena. Keep the noted seaweed stack (one slot): one seaweed becomes the soda ash for the molten glass you make after the sandstone later in this section, and the rest of the stack is dropped then. Open any caskets for their coins. If a tackle box appears, drop it for now: you carry no fishing tools in this section, and it rerolls in later pool visits. Drop everything else as it arrives, including the feathers and bait (nothing in this section fly fishes; 1.4 buys a rod and feathers in Port Sarim for Death Plateau's trout), spirit flakes and soaked pages.

            Watch the slot count while the rewards come in. Each new item type takes its own slot until you drop it, so pause the searching and clear the junk whenever you get down to three or four free slots, rather than letting a roll arrive with nowhere to go.

            The raw fish are noted. When you want to eat, unnote a handful at any bank booth and cook them on the nearest range; that is the one thing a UIM still does at a bank.

            Done when the pool is collected.

            Pick up a saw on the way to Kourend

            You dropped your saw in 1.2. Break a Chronicle card to land at the Champions' Guild, walk north to the estate agent's house in north-east Varrock and take the saw that spawns inside. Cast Lumbridge Home Teleport and walk west through Draynor to Port Sarim, where Veos sails you to Port Piscarilius. While you're at the dock, claim your destroyed X Marks the Spot lamp from Veos and use it on Herblore.

            The Chronicle drops you just outside the guild, and the spawn is free, which is why the guide fetches the saw here instead of paying for one: the sawmill operator in the Woodcutting Guild sells saws for 13 gp, so if someone has taken the Varrock spawn, buy one there instead of waiting. You took the carpet back to the Shantay Pass rather than using Lumbridge Home Teleport for the pool, and that is why the teleport is ready now: it has a 30-minute cooldown, and you need it here. Veos is on the Port Sarim docks, south of the Rusty Anchor Inn.

            Done when you hold a saw and are in Kourend.

            Coins: about 275,000 gp · Slots: about 24/28 with the plank, nail, swordfish and seaweed stacks and the saw · Requirements: none

            Wiki references Reward pool (Tempoross) Saw Veos

            Construction to 46 in Hosidius

            This block takes Construction from about 30 to 46 and builds a costume room with an oak armour case. It is the most important UIM investment in Chapter 1. The armour case holds full sets of skilling and minigame outfits; for a UIM it accepts pieces one at a time and only gives them back once the set is complete, which is exactly how the Rogues' Den hands them out. The wiki's own advice for UIMs doing the Rogues' Den is a Taverley house with an armour case, and that is the plan.

            Gate in 33 Construction for oak larders, 42 for the costume room, 46 for the oak armour case; the pool planks.
            Carry in the plank stacks and food: about 24/28.
            Exit 46 Construction; 52 Cooking from the mess hall; the costume room with an oak armour case; the Kourend lamps claimed; the house moved to Taverley; about 35,000 gp.
            Carries forward the costume room (rogue outfit, angler's outfit).

            The training happens in Hosidius because the Woodcutting Guild's sawmill is a short run from the Hosidius house portal, so a trip is: logs, planks, house, larders, and back.

            The costume room's UIM rule cuts both ways: you can't store a duplicate piece, and you can't take anything out of a set until every piece of it has gone in. That is why the angler's outfit, which you already own whole, can go in and come out freely, while the rogue outfit stays locked in the case until the fifth piece arrives. The oak case holds up to 25 sets, far more than this guide ever puts in it; the graceful outfit goes into a magic wardrobe in the same room later in this section.

            Move the house and claim the Kourend lamps

            On the Port Piscarilius dock, claim the two Client of Kourend lamps from Veos and use them on Herblore too. Walk south to Hosidius and pay the estate agent 8,750 gp to move your house to Hosidius.

            The two lamps are worth 500 XP each. The Hosidius move needs 25 Construction and can't be boosted; you passed 30 in 1.1, so it is open to you, and you have already visited Kourend, which the agent also checks.

            Done when the house is in Hosidius and the lamps are used.

            Cooking to 52 at the Hosidius mess hall

            You are in Hosidius with about 43 Cooking, and two later steps need more: the raw swordfish from the Tempoross pool, your food for the rest of the section, need 45 to cook, and the botanical pie for Eadgar's Ruse in 1.4 needs 52. The mess hall just south of Kingstown is the UIM way to close that gap, because the wiki says it needs no gathered materials: the flour, potatoes, meat and the dishes and bowls all come from its cupboards and tables, and it no longer needs Kourend favour. Make servery stews (25 Cooking, about 168 XP each, about 60,000 to 69,000 XP an hour at lower levels by the wiki's estimate) with your free slots, serve them, and repeat until 52, about 73,000 XP or a little over an hour. Drop whatever ingredients are left when you stop.

            Done when you are 52 Cooking.

            Coins: unchanged · Slots: your free slots for the ingredients · Requirements: 25 Cooking for servery stew

            Spend the pool planks first

            Unnote 24 oak planks at a bank booth. Marlo's five house tablets are used up by now, so walk into the Hosidius house portal instead (it is a short walk), build a kitchen (5,000 gp, level 5) next to the parlour, and build an oak larder in it (level 33, 8 oak planks, 480 XP). Remove it and build it again until the planks run out, then fetch the next 24. When the oak planks are gone, build wooden bookcases in the parlour from the regular planks and steel nails (4 planks and 4 nails each, 115 XP) the same way.

            Keep the noted stacks noted between trips and unnote only what fits in your free slots.

            Done when the pool planks are used.

            Oak larders from the Woodcutting Guild

            When the pool's planks are used up, the rest comes from oak logs. Wield the steel axe in place of the blackjack and fill every free slot with oak logs: about nine at first, because the noted stacks, saw, hammer and coins stay with you, and about fourteen once the angler's outfit is in the armour case. Give the logs to the sawmill operator in the Woodcutting Guild for oak planks (250 gp each), run to the house portal, go in and build larders with every set of eight planks. Run back from the portal and repeat. This is the slowest stretch of the chapter, roughly 70 trips and two to three hours of play; nothing here is dangerous, so put a podcast on.

            The guild needs 60 Woodcutting to enter, which Wintertodt gave you in 1.1, and the sawmill is on its east side.

            At 42 Construction, build a costume room (50,000 gp). At 46, build an oak armour case in it (3 oak planks). Take the angler's outfit off and use it on the case right away: it is four fewer things to carry for the rest of the chapter.

            From 30 to 46 is about 54,600 XP; the pool's planks give about 14,000, and an oak larder gives 60 XP per plank, so you need roughly 680 oak planks on top of the pool's, depending on what it gave you. That is about 170,000 gp of sawmill fees, which is what the first blackjacking session paid for.

            Done when you are 46 Construction with the costume room and oak armour case built.

            Move the house to Taverley

            Before you spend the pool planks, set aside four planks and eighteen oak planks and keep them noted: the planks wait for the Observatory and Mountain Daughter in 1.4, and the oak planks go into your magic wardrobe later in this section (four), and the workshop's crafting table (four) and the Museum Camp on Fossil Island (ten) at the start of 1.4. Noted, they are two slots; unnote them at a bank booth right before each build. When the larders are done, drop the steel nails and the saw: the saw that spawns in the Varrock estate agent's house is free when the wardrobe needs one. At Vannah's farm shop in Hosidius, buy a compost pack (2,000 gp), a rake and a seed dibber, open the pack, and leave all of it with the tool leprechaun at the Hosidius allotment; the leprechaun hands them back at any farming patch, and 1.4's compost line and the barley at the hops patch north of Lumbridge need them. Walk back to the Hosidius estate agent and move the house to Taverley for 5,000 gp. Taverley is a short walk from the Rogues' Den in Burthorpe, which is the whole point of the next block.

            Vannah's stall is in the Hosidius farmer's market, and the rake and seed dibber cost 6 gp each there.

            Done when your house is in Taverley.

            Coins: about 35,000 gp · Slots: about 20/28 with the plank and food stacks · Requirements: 33 Construction for oak larders, 42 for the costume room, 46 for the oak armour case

            Wiki references Costume room Armour case space Oak larder Woodcutting Guild Estate agent

            The rogue outfit

            The Rogues' Den maze needs an empty inventory and nothing worn, and each successful run ends at a wall safe that gives a Rogue's equipment crate, from which you choose any one piece of the set (or a rogue kit). Because you choose, there are no duplicates, but a failed lockpick at the safe gives nothing, so the set takes at least five successful runs. This block uses the section's first planned death to empty your inventory for about an hour, stores each piece in the Taverley armour case as it arrives, and collects everything at the end.

            Gate in 50 Agility and 50 Thieving; an empty inventory and nothing worn (a Falador Castle deathpile).
            Carry in nothing for the maze; then about 24/28.
            Exit the full rogue outfit in the armour case; a pickaxe and soft clay; about 30,000 gp.
            Carries forward the rogue outfit (double blackjacking loot).

            The outfit pays for itself many times over. Each worn piece adds 15% to the chance of doubling a pickpocket's loot, and the full five-piece set makes it certain, which is why the guide waits for all five before blackjacking again: four pieces give only 60%.

            Planned death: Falador Castle

            Sail back to Port Sarim with Veos and walk north to Falador. Don't use a minigame teleport on the way: you need one the moment you respawn, and they share a 20-minute cooldown.

            In the courtyard of Falador Castle, take off your staff, turn auto-retaliate off and attack a White Knight. Let it kill you. Keep any fish you carry noted: the wiki says unnoted food disappears from a deathpile and doesn't say whether raw fish count, so the guide never risks it. Note the time. The wiki lists Falador Castle as a deathpile spot: the White Knights don't wander, nothing else there is aggressive, and the castle is a two-minute walk from Taverley. Your whole inventory drops where you die, hidden from everyone else. It stays for 60 minutes of logged-in time, and the timer pauses if you log out.

            UIM risk The Chronicle can't go into the pile, so this death destroys it; its charges are saved, and you buy it back in Draynor on the way to the Wizards' Tower after the den.

            Before you attack, check that the angler's outfit really is in the armour case and that everything else you own is in your inventory or worn: the pile is the only place it can be for the next hour.

            Done when your pile is in the courtyard.

            Five or more maze runs

            You respawn in Lumbridge with nothing. Use the Burthorpe Games Room minigame teleport, which needs no items, and walk to the Toad and Chicken pub. The trapdoor behind the bar leads to the Rogues' Den; you meet its 50 Agility and 50 Thieving requirement.

            Talk to Brian O'Richard at the entrance first: he has to introduce the maze before your first run. Run the maze. It is a fixed obstacle course of traps, ledges, pressure plates and doors to pick. At the end, crack one of the wall safes; which safe you pick makes no difference. Open the Rogue's equipment crate it gives and choose a piece you don't have yet. Each run also gives 12 Agility and Thieving XP per level drained. Walk out of the den and south to your house portal in Taverley, put the piece in the armour case, and walk back.

            The maze can't hurt you. Its traps and obstacles deal no damage; a mistake drains a few levels of Agility or Thieving instead, and if either level is drained below zero you are put back in the lobby to start again. Your Prayer drops to zero inside, and a mystic jewel in your inventory takes you back to the lobby at any time, so a run that has gone wrong costs only time. Flash powder blinds the Rogue Guards long enough for you to run past them. Cracking the end safe sends you back to the lobby whether it succeeds or fails, so a failed crack means another full run.

            Between runs, while your inventory is still empty, crack one of the wall safes in the den's main room for the diary. It works without a stethoscope.

            That safe is a Falador hard diary task (50 Thieving), and only the lobby safes count, not the ones at the end of the maze. It can give uncut gems or a few coins; drop the gems, since nothing in this section uses them. Chapter 3 claims the Falador hard diary, and this task is then already done.

            When the fifth different piece goes into the case, the set is complete and you can take it out again. Wear it.

            Done when you hold the full rogue outfit.

            Collect the deathpile

            Walk from Taverley to Falador Castle and pick everything up except the spade and the clue hunter gloves: nothing in this section digs, and the rogue gloves, and later the graceful gloves, take your hands slot. Check the clock as you go: from your death to here should take about 40 minutes. If you are running late, skip the safe and go straight to the pile.

            While you're in Falador in the rogue outfit, pickpocket a Falador guard once for the diary, and do one lap of the Falador rooftop course for the diary as well.

            The guard is a medium Falador task and needs 40 Thieving; the rooftop lap is a hard task and needs 50 Agility. You meet both with room to spare, and the rogue outfit doubles whatever the guard gives you.

            Done when your pile is collected.

            A pickaxe and soft clay

            The next part of the section needs a pickaxe. Walk to the Dwarven Mine and buy an adamant pickaxe from Nurmof (it needs 31 Mining to use, which The Dig Site gives you later) and a bronze pickaxe for 1 gp to mine the clay with now; a rune pickaxe costs ten times as much and mines nothing here faster that matters for a UIM. Walk south to Rimmington, mine four clay, buy a bucket at the general store and make four soft clay at the well, then drop the bronze pickaxe and the bucket. The Golem needs the clay.

            Nurmof's shop is in the north-west corner of the Dwarven Mine, down the alley with the anvil south-west of the north entrance. The adamant pickaxe costs 3,200 gp, and he keeps only two in stock, so if both are gone, hop worlds rather than settling for the rune pickaxe at 32,000.

            Make the soft clay last: it is four slots, and The Golem uses it in the next block.

            Done when you hold a pickaxe and soft clay.

            Coins: about 30,000 gp · Slots: about 23/28 with the soft clay · Requirements: 50 Agility and 50 Thieving for the Rogues' Den

            Wiki references Rogues' Den Rogue equipment Ultimate Ironman Guide/Minigames Ultimate Ironman Guide/Item Management Nurmof

            Rune Mysteries and the road to Pollnivneach

            Rogue Trader, the miniquest that opens Ali Morrisane's rune shop, needs Rune Mysteries finished, and Ali has to send you to Aubury himself. This block finishes Rune Mysteries on the way south, gets Ali's first dialogue, starts The Golem at Uzer with the soft clay you just made, and runs the main blackjacking session in the rogue outfit.

            Gate in The Feud, Rune Mysteries and Gertrude's Cat for Rogue Trader; 20 Crafting and 25 Thieving for The Golem; 65 Thieving for Menaphite thugs.
            Carry in about 23/28.
            Exit Rune Mysteries done; Rogue Trader and The Golem started; about 910,000 gp after blackjacking.
            Carries forward the coins for Ali's runes.

            Rune Mysteries

            Walk from Rimmington to Port Sarim and on to Draynor, buy the Chronicle back from Diango in the market for 300 gp (its teleport charges come back with it) and twelve teleport cards (1,800 gp), one for each Chronicle teleport from here to the end of the chapter, and continue to the Wizards' Tower, and give the research notes to Sedridor in the basement to complete Rune Mysteries (since November 2026 it also pays 500 Runecraft XP). That frees the slot the notes have held since 1.1.

            The tower is south of Draynor; climb down the ladder to find Sedridor. Completing the quest also lets Aubury teleport you to the rune essence mine, which is how you collect Drezel's 50 essence for Priest in Peril in the Morytania block. The quest is worth 5 kudos at the Varrock Museum, which you claim with the others later in this section.

            You carry no fishing rod yet: the fly rod and feathers are bought on your next pass through Port Sarim, and the Lumbridge salmon for the diary waits until then.

            Done when Rune Mysteries is complete.

            Start Rogue Trader

            Pay the 10 gp toll at the Al Kharid gate and talk to Ali Morrisane, east of the gem trader, to start Rogue Trader. He sends you to Aubury in Varrock; you go there in the block after next, when you have the money to buy what Ali sells.

            When he asks, choose the option asking what's in it for you: starting Rogue Trader halves the magic carpet fare from 200 to 100 gp, which matters with the carpet rides between the Shantay Pass, Uzer and Pollnivneach over the next few blocks.

            Done when Rogue Trader is started.

            The Golem

            Buy a Shantay pass at the Shantay Pass, along with the three desert clothes and a few waterskins if you dropped them, go through the gate and walk east across the desert to the Ruins of Uzer. The carpet to Uzer only opens once The Golem is complete, and there is no carpet from Pollnivneach. Talk to the clay golem to start The Golem and use your four soft clay on it to repair it. Ask how to open the portal, read the letter outside the temple, then go down the stairs behind the golem, and take the strange implement from the north-western room; the black mushroom by the stairs waits until you finish the quest here, since the wiki's quick guide grinds it only at the end. The quest then sends you to the Digsite and the Varrock Museum, which is a later block. Before you leave, chop a teak log from the tree behind the ruins for the diary (35 Woodcutting), then drop it.

            The walk to Uzer is in the desert heat, so wear all three desert pieces: each delays the heat damage on its own, and full waterskins drink themselves automatically when the heat would otherwise hurt you. The golem takes the clay one piece at a time, so use each of the four on it until it is repaired. Nothing in the quest has to be fought: the demon behind the portal died long before you arrive. Keep the strange implement safe; you use it on the golem, followed by the program, when you come back to finish.

            Done when The Golem is started.

            Blackjack to 910,000 gp

            Walk back to the Shantay Pass and take the carpet to Pollnivneach. Drop the desert clothes and waterskins there (the Shantay Pass sells fresh ones next time), wear the full rogue outfit and blackjack the Menaphite thugs at the southern end of town; you already have the 65 Thieving they need. With the full set, every successful pickpocket pays double.

            Each successful pickpocket on a thug pays 60 coins and 137.5 Thieving XP, so with the full set you collect 120 coins a time. The thugs are level 55 and not aggressive until you fail a knock-out. If one does turn on you, knock it out again straight away, which resets its aggression; if that fails, get it stuck on an object in the room and knock it out from there, or run off and come back. Keep the inventory full of wine, as with the bandits, so you can't pickpocket a conscious thug by mistake.

            Stop at 920,000 gp.
